# Accuracy log

What was checked, how, and what is still open. Lesson-level detail (claims, references, flags) lives in each lesson JSON and in the app under **Evidence & accuracy**. Reviewer-level findings live in `content/reviewer-review.json` and on the **Coverage** page.

**No human expert has reviewed any content.** Nothing here is endorsed by ASCP, the ASCP BOC, or the PRC.

## Policy

- The reviewer (*MEMORY LAB: The Memory-First Medical Laboratory Science Reviewer*, A. Almoradie, 1st ed. v2.0, 2026) is the **curriculum foundation**, not an authority.
- A claim is `checked` only if it cites at least one **external** source that was actually read. The validator rejects claims whose only support is the reviewer (`kind: "reviewer"`).
- The reviewer's own release record says it had **no independent human specialist review**. It reports 22 items flagged for human review and a 34-item review queue; neither is in the EPUB. Ask the author for them.

## External sources consulted

| Source | Used for |
|---|---|
| R.A. 5527, Sec. 15–19 (lawphil.net) | MTLE subjects and weights; passing rule as enacted |
| PRB-MT Res. No. 13, s. 2023 (prc.gov.ph) | Enhanced TOS adopted from Aug 2023 (Annex A not retrieved) |
| R.A. 4688 (lawphil.net) | PH clinical-laboratory registration, annual DOH license, physician in charge |
| 42 CFR 493.3, 493.5, 493.15, 493.551 (eCFR) | CLIA applicability and exclusions, complexity categories and certificates, waived tests, deemed status |
| CMS, *How to Obtain a CLIA Certificate*; CMS *List of Exempt States* v. 08/15/2025 | FDA categorization, stringency rises with complexity; WA (full) and NY (partial) exempt |

| AABB Standards page | 35th edition effective April 1, 2026 |

| Haferlach et al., *Med Genet* 2024;36:3 | WHO-HAEM5 blast-threshold changes |

- The official ASCP BOC MLS content guideline PDF (HTTP 403/404). ASCP weights remain unverified. The reviewer's Appendix A says 17–22% for the four major areas; one secondary source said 17–20% for Microbiology.
- Annex A of PRB-MT Res. 13 s. 2023 (HTTP 403).
- NCBI Bookshelf (CAPTCHA), the AABB Technical Manual, and Harmening (not available).
- The reviewer's three mock-exam PDFs (not provided).

## Reviewer accuracy register (summary)

There are 552 flags: 419 verified issues and 133 that still need verification. The full text, with evidence links, is in `content/reviewer-review.json`.

Verified:
- **Ch. 1 Trap 2:** exempt states given as "New York, D.C."; CMS lists Washington and New York.
- **Ch. 40 Vault #1:** "35 ISBT systems"; ISBT now lists 48 systems and 371 antigens.
- **Bibliography and Ch. 74:** AABB Standards 2019; the current edition is the 35th (2026).
- **Bibliography:** CLSI M100 2020; the current edition is Ed36 (2026). Ch. 58 breakpoints may be outdated.
- **Ch. 26:** WHO 2010 semen values; the WHO 6th edition (2021) revised them, and the reviewer's 2–5 mL volume doesn't match WHO 2010 either.
- **Ch. 35:** blast threshold ≥20% per WHO 2017; WHO-HAEM5 and the 2022 ICC differ.
- **Ch. 42 Hit 2:** TPE removal percentages (30% / 10% left) contradict its own e⁻ˣ formula (≈37% / 14%).
- **Ch. 22:** HBV chronicity "80% neonates, 1–2% adults" (CDC: ~90% infants, ~5% adults); IgM anti-HBc called the "sole" window marker (total anti-HBc and HBV DNA too).
- **Ch. 26 Hits 1–2:** fixed hCG discriminatory zone presented as diagnostic context (Bobdiwala 2017: varies, never diagnoses ectopic alone); “< 53% rise in 48 h = abnormal” (newer data: 35% minimum for a viable IUP).
- **Ch. 18 Trap 2:** direct LDL assay said to “stay valid even at TG 600” (HEART UK/ACB 2025: method-dependent, marked bias vs the reference method, not recommended in hypertriglyceridaemia).
- **Ch. 41 Hit 4:** BNP said to “NOT rise” in TRALI and TRALI treated with “oxygen only” (Vlaar 2019: natriuretic peptides are always raised in severe TRALI and critical illness; care is supportive, including ventilation).
- **Ch. 33 Hit 5:** osmotic fragility listed as an HS test without noting the conventional OFT is no longer recommended (Chueh 2022: flow-based OFT and EMA binding preferred).
- **Ch. 23 Hit 2:** acute pancreatitis defined as clinical features + enzymes > 3 × ULN (revised Atlanta: any two of pain, enzymes ≥ 3 × ULN, imaging).
- **Ch. 63 Trap 5:** granulocytic morulae equated with Anaplasma only (Biggs 2016: E. ewingii also infects granulocytes; smears need confirmation).
- **Ch. 41 Hit 5:** “< 8.0 g/dL in acute coronary syndrome” (MINT: restrictive 7–8 g/dL may increase MI or death in acute MI).
- **Ch. 41 Hit 7:** cryo trigger fibrinogen < 100 mg/dL and 150–250 mg/unit (current reviews: risk rises below ~200 mg/dL; AABB ≥ 150 mg/unit; triggers vary).
- **Ch. 36 Hit 5:** mild hemophilia given as 6–30% (standard: > 5–40 IU/dL).
- **Ch. 19 Hits 1, 3, 5:** built on the Fourth Universal Definition of MI; the Fifth (2026) requires sex-specific 99th percentile URLs and replaces numbered types with primary, secondary and procedure-related MI.
- **Ch. 58 Trap 5:** “aztreonam stays active” against MBL producers (Khan 2026: co-produced ESBL/AmpC often inactivate it).
- **Ch. 42 Hits 15–16:** 12.5 g/dL / 38% said to apply to all whole-blood donors (21 CFR 630.10: male allogeneic ≥ 13.0 g/dL / 39%); lower BP limits omitted.
- **Ch. 57 Hit 29:** Enterococcus called a “β-hemolytic streptococcus” (separate genus; β-hemolysis in only 20% of isolates, Fialho 2026). Confirm by 6.5% NaCl and bile esculin.
- **Ch. 37 Hit 3:** VWF activity/antigen and FVIII/antigen cut-off “0.6” (2021 ASH ISTH NHF WFH guideline, via Tosetto 2026: < 0.7, low certainty).
- **Ch. 15 Hit 3:** normal anion gap “≈ 12 (8–16)” (method-dependent: ion-selective electrodes ≈ 6 ± 3, Lee 2006; a 2025 review lists 4–12).
- **Ch. 66 Hit 4:** sputum rejected at > 10 SEC/LPF but “acceptable if < 25 epithelial cells” (self-contradictory; Dong 2026: < 10 SEC + > 25 leukocytes).
- **Ch. 41 Hit 14:** HBV “core window” said to be caught by NAT (Badawi 2025: anti-HBc may be the only marker there; NAT shortens the early window).
- **Ch. 29 Hit 1:** RBC casts “glomerular disease, full stop” (Gaggar 2024: RBC casts in 29% of biopsy-proven interstitial nephritis).
- **Ch. 29 Hit 5:** WBC casts tied to pyelonephritis and waxy casts to end-stage disease (Gaggar 2024: WBC casts also in interstitial nephritis and nephritic syndrome; waxy casts in acute and chronic renal failure).
- **Ch. 34 Hit 4:** PV “JAK2 V617F in essentially all cases” (> 95%; 3–5% carry exon 12–15 mutations — Palandri 2026, Testa 2026).
- **Ch. 34 Hit 5:** PMF median survival “~5 years” (Shao 2025: 9.2 years in a large series).
- **Ch. 38 Hits 3 and 9:** factor V Leiden fold-risks (homozygous “10–15×”, + OCPs “8–20×”) conflict with the reviewer’s own Ch. 72 simulator question (~80× homozygous) and with published figures (Khider 2022: ~10× homozygous; Nakashima & Rogers 2014: 80× homozygous, 30–60× with OCPs).
- **Ch. 20 Trap 2:** bisalbuminemia called a benign variant needing no work-up; Kapatia 2021 also describes a transient acquired form (high-dose β-lactams, pancreatic pseudocyst).
- **Ch. 60 Hit 9 / Trap 4:** piperacillin-tazobactam presented as a current cause of false-positive galactomannan (Vergidis 2014, abstract: 0 of 32 current US lots contained GM; now a rare cause).
- **Ch. 60 simulator Q786:** *A. niger* described as uniseriate (Samson 2007: biseriate).
- **Ch. 40 simulator Q372:** RhIG for a 60 mL bleed given as 2 vials; technical guidance (Hajjaj 2025) rounds and adds one vial (3).
- **Ch. 57 Hit 36:** HACEK called “culture-negative” (Khaledi 2022: ~99% detected within 5 days by automated blood culture; prolonged incubation of no value). Ch. 57 Hit 33 repeats the *A. niger* “uniseriate” error (rv-niger-seriation).
- **Ch. 36 Hit 14 / Trap 6:** platelets “> 20 × 10⁹/L” in bleeding DIC (Wada 2014, summarizing BCSH/JSTH/SISET/ISTH: transfuse bleeding DIC at ≤ 50 × 10⁹/L; 10–20 only for non-bleeding).
- **Ch. 17 Hit 3 / Trap 3:** high insulin + high C-peptide called insulinoma without excluding sulfonylureas (ENETS 2012; Peltola 2018).
- **Ch. 42 Hit 8:** plerixafor described as only for NHL/myeloma patients “failing G-CSF” (De Clercq 2019: approved 2008 for autologous mobilization in NHL/myeloma, with G-CSF).
- **Ch. 30 Hit 5:** septic arthritis defined by WBC 50,000–200,000/µL and low glucose (Long 2019: counts overlap with crystal arthritis; ~half of septic joints ≤ 28,000/µL; glucose unhelpful).
- **Ch. 72 Hit 4:** Huntington 36–39 CAG called “intermediate” (Nopoulos 2016; Chintalaphani 2021: 36–39 is reduced penetrance; 27–35 is the non-disease range that may expand).
- **Ch. 72 Hit 5:** congenital DM1 “transmitted maternally only” (Wenninger 2018: characteristically maternal, but paternal transmission is known; healthy 5–37, penetrant > 50).
- **Ch. 57 Hit 40:** Shiga toxin detected “directly in stool by EIA” (Humphries 2015; CDC 2009: EIA on enrichment broth; direct stool ~70% sensitivity).
- **Ch. 64 Hit 14:** p24 antigen called “the earliest detectable HIV marker” (CDC 2014: HIV-1 RNA appears first; p24 4–10 days later).
- **Ch. 16 Hit 4:** corrected calcium with a 4.4 g/dL reference albumin; simulator calls 4.0 “outdated” (Desgagnés 2025; Mirrakhimov 2015: the commonly used simplified Payne formula uses 4.0 g/dL).
- **Ch. 77 Hit 2:** “MR2 = 10%” (ELN 2020: 1% IS = 2 logs; 10% = 1 log).
- **Ch. 76 Hit 1:** blanket “95–100% specificity” for oncoprotein assays (NMP22 BladderChek pooled specificity 88%).
- **Ch. 76 Hit 4:** KRAS “~75% of colon cancers” (Simanshu 2017 Table 1: 42%).
- **Ch. 73 Hit 7 / Trap 2:** “ACE inhibition is a risk factor for AD” (observational evidence only; RCT meta-analysis non-significant — Belachew 2025).
- **Ch. 50 Hit 4:** positive CDC crossmatch called an “absolute contraindication” (DTT-negative IgM/autoreactive positives are standard risk; lupus false positives — Althaf 2017).
- **Ch. 52 Hit 6:** CVID “age > 2 years” (1999 ESID/PAGID); ICON 2016 advises not before age 4.
- **Ch. 55 Hit 1:** anti-EMA as the confirmation test (ESsCD 2025: routine IgA-EMA confirmation no longer recommended in adults).
- **Ch. 51 Hit 11:** “relative risk = (a×d)/(b×c)” (that is the odds ratio).
- **Ch. 51 Hit 6:** HFE penetrance “1–10%” (definition-dependent; eMERGE 24.4% men, 14.0% women diagnosed).
- **Ch. 12 Hit 2:** ACID Isolation defined as “concurrent transactions behave as sequential” (that is only the serializable level; PostgreSQL defaults to Read Committed).
- **Ch. 13 Hit 3:** contribution margin defined as price − fully loaded cost (standard definition: price − variable cost).
- **Ch. 13 Trap 4:** positive NPV said to need a separate IRR check (for a conventional project the two tests agree).
- **Ch. 14 Hit 3:** “the IRB, not the investigator, decides exemption” (institutional policy; not stated in 45 CFR 46.104; needs verification).
- **Ch. 58 Hit 8:** Describes vanB as 'lower-level vancomycin resistance'. (`rv-vanb-teicoplanin`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 58 Hit 12:** Gives a single streptomycin high-level screen of 1000 µg/mL. The cutoff depends on method: >1,000 µg/mL by broth dilution and >2,000 µg/mL by agar dilution (Shridhar 2019, citing CLSI). (`rv-hlar-streptomycin-method`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 58 Trap 3:** States that oxacillin susceptibility 'only rules out mecA'. (`rv-oxacillin-s-mecc`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 58 Hit 6:** Says to screen penicillin-susceptible S. aureus with nitrocefin. (`rv-mssa-penicillinase-zone-edge`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 59 Hit 6:** Hit 6 and the Number Vault give Xpert MTB/RIF sensitivity as 98-100% in smear-positive and 69-72% in smear-negative sputum, and the Number Vault gives specificity as 100%. (`rv-xpert-pooled-accuracy`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 59 Hit 8:** Hit 8 (RIPE regimen; test every initial isolate to all four primary drugs) matches the US ATS/CDC/IDSA 2016 treatment guideline (preferred adult regimen of 2 months INH, RIF, PZA, EMB then 4 months INH and RIF; DST for INH, RIF, EMB and PZA should be obtained). (`rv-tb-dst-us-guideline`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 68 Hit 9:** Hit 9 says Southern and northern blots are 'both largely replaced by amplification methods'. (`rv-blots-still-used`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 18 Hit 4:** Hit 4 presents type 3 dysbetalipoproteinemia as an 'equal cholesterol and TG elevation' with tuberoeruptive xanthomas, which reads as a pattern you can recognise from a standard panel. (`rv-fd-lipid-pattern`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 18 Hit 10:** Hit 10 states that apoC-II deficiency functionally mimics LPL deficiency. (`rv-apoc2-milder`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 65 Hit 5:** Hit 5 (and Exam Trap 1) say an antigen test MUST be used to distinguish E. histolytica from E. dispar. (`rv-entamoeba-antigen-only`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 65 Hit 8:** Hit 8 (and the Number Vault) give Cystoisospora belli oocysts as ~25 x 33 um, implying a width near 25 um. (`rv-cystoisospora-size`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 31 Hit 11:** The reviewer says an ESR >100 mm/h in a known cancer patient usually means metastases. (`rv-esr-100-cancer`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 31 Hit 11:** The reviewer says temporal arteritis/polymyalgia rheumatica typically run >90 mm/h. (`rv-esr-gca-90`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 27 Trap 4:** Trap 4 says functional tests (erythrocyte Cu-Zn superoxide dismutase activity) are more meaningful than serum copper. (`rv-copper-sod-marker`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 27 Trap 6:** Trap 6 says plasma is preferred, and the Number Vault trick says serum runs 5-10% higher than plasma. (`rv-zinc-serum-plasma`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 27 Hit 3:** Hit 3 gives low serum copper (RBC copper normal) for Menkes syndrome. (`rv-menkes-neonatal-copper`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 72 Hit 7:** Hit 7 (and the Number Vault) give factor V Leiden as 5-7% of whites, ~7x heterozygous risk and 'lifetime penetrance only ~10%'. (`rv-fvl-ch72-penetrance`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 72 Trap 3:** Trap 3 (and the Number Vault) call the MTHFR 677C>T thrombotic association 'questionable' and the test 'largely dropped'. (`rv-mthfr-not-thrombophilia`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 25 Hit 1:** acromegaly said to be “diagnosed by” OGTT GH non-suppression (Giustina 2024 consensus: IGF-I > 1.3 × ULN plus clinical signs confirms it; the OGTT is for equivocal results).
- **Ch. 62 Hit 3:** chlamydiae “can’t make ATP” (Mandel 2024; Cheong 2023: RBs scavenge host ATP, but EBs and C. trachomatis can generate ATP).
- **Ch. 62 Trap 4:** azithromycin and doxycycline presented as equal first-line chlamydia treatment (CDC 2021: doxycycline recommended; azithromycin alternative).
- **Ch. 41 Hit 10:** platelets stored “up to 5 days” (Akaraphanth 2026: 5–7 days at room temperature, plus an FDA 2023 variance for cold-stored platelets up to 14 days at 1–6 °C for active bleeding).
- **Ch. 24 Trap 3:** dilute urine given as creatinine ≤ 20 mg/dL and SG ≤ 1.0030 (DOT 40.88: creatinine ≥ 2 and < 20, SG > 1.0010 and < 1.0030 — the reviewer's numbers match clinical tampering flags); out-of-range temperature listed as a substitution criterion (DOT 40.65: a collection check that triggers an observed recollection).
- **Ch. 15 core:** "FENa > 1% suggests ATN"; a 2025 consensus treats 1–2% as indeterminate and > 2% as intrinsic.
- **Ch. 66 core:** blood-culture yields "80 / 96 / 100%" for 2 / 3 / 4 sets; Lee 2007 found 90 / 98 / 99.8%.
- **Ch. 31 core:** "MCHC increased **only** in spherocytosis"; xerocytosis also raises it, and cold agglutinins and interference raise it spuriously.
- **Ch. 57 Hit 3:** "tube coagulase positive = *S. aureus*"; ASM notes *S. schleiferi* and *S. intermedius* may also be tube-positive.
- **Ch. 1 and 13:** US-only regulation with no PH equivalents (context gap for the MTLE track).

Needs verification:
- **Ch. 43 Hit 12:** “HPCs do not express ABO antigens”; Matteocci 2024 states ABO antigens are present on HSCs. Primary CD34⁺ expression studies not read.

- **Ch. 57 Hit 45:** Hit 45 says MALDI-TOF 'does NOT do susceptibility testing'. (`rv-maldi-ast`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 66 Hit 42:** The reviewer gives a fixed list of microbiology critical values (positive CSF Gram stain, positive blood cultures, positive AFB smear, positive malaria smear) and a single read-back procedure. (`rv-micro-critical-list-local`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 66 Hit 41,42,43,44:** The duties for record content, immediate critical alerts and corrected reports are US federal CLIA requirements (42 CFR 493.1241, 493.1283, 493.1291). (`rv-micro-reporting-us-law`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 4 Hit 15:** Hit 15 (and Exam Trap 5) says SDS-PAGE separates proteins by molecular mass 'alone'. (`rv-sds-page-mass-anomalies`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 4 Hit 17:** Hit 17's 'large molecules elute first' is correct as the ideal rule. (`rv-sec-stokes-radius`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 47 Hit 11:** Hit 11 says the MS workup uses 'IFE' on unconcentrated CSF paired with serum, without naming isoelectric focusing. (`rv-ocb-ief-method`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 47 Hit 9:** Hit 9 says oligoclonal-band types 2 and 3 mean intrathecal IgG synthesis 'supporting multiple sclerosis'. (`rv-ocb-not-ms-specific`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 75 Hit 7:** Hit 7 (and the Number Vault/Memory Hooks) states exon 19 deletions plus exon 21 L858R are together >90% of EGFR mutations. (`rv-egfr-common-share`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 75 Hit 8:** Hit 8 and Exam Trap 4 state BRAF mutation is an anti-EGFR resistance marker in colorectal cancer. (`rv-crc-braf-predictive`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 75 Hit 7,8,13:** Hits 7, 8 and 13 rely on US FDA decisions (osimertinib accelerated approval 2015, FDA labels requiring wild-type RAS by an FDA-approved test, crizotinib 2011 with Vysis ALK FISH companion diagnostic). (`rv-ch75-us-approvals`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 77 Hit 7:** Hit 7 (and Exam Trap 1) explains the 60-70% PCR detection of t(14;18) as 30-40% of breakpoints falling outside the MBR. The open sources give the MBR as 50-65% and the mcr as 10-20% of breakpoints. (`rv-ch77-t1418-pcr-coverage`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 77 Hit 8:** Hit 8 gives a binary IGHV cut-off (unmutated <=2% divergence) and says high-risk CLL needs BTK/BCL2 inhibitors. (`rv-ch77-ighv-borderline`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 77 Hit 11:** Hit 11 gives MYD88 L265P in >90% of LPL/WM. Western series report MYD88 mutation (almost always L265P) in about 93-97%, but a large Chinese cohort found 87.8%. (`rv-ch77-myd88-not-exclusive`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 8 Trap 4:** Trap 4 says the most likely cause of a wild potassium delta-check swing is preanalytic mislabelling. (`rv-ch8-delta-check-causes`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 8 Trap 5:** Trap 5 (and Hit 9) give the troponin URL as the 99th percentile of a normal population. (`rv-ch8-troponin-sex-specific`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 8 Trap 6:** Trap 6 and the Number Vault give an HbA1c laboratory range of 4.0–6.0% against the diabetes threshold of 6.5% or higher. (`rv-ch8-hba1c-range-overlap`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 53 Hit 7:** Hit 7 presents anti-HMGCR as the marker of statin-associated necrotizing myopathy. (`rv-hmgcr-statin-naive`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 53 Hit 15:** Hit 15 states anti-Ro52/TRIM21 with anti-ARS means more severe myositis and joint impairment plus increased cancer and ILD risk. (`rv-ro52-ars-muscle`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 53 Hit 9:** Hit 9 gives anti-NT5c1A a specificity above 90% for sIBM. Specificity depends on assay, cutoff and comparator group. (`rv-nt5c1a-specificity`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 23 Hit 13:** Hit 13 presents fecal elastase-1 200 μg/g as the cut-off that detects pancreatic insufficiency. (`rv-fe1-soft-cutoff`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 23 Trap 6:** Trap 6 (and Hit 14) state that ~2% of coeliac patients have selective IgA deficiency and advise IgG-based serology (DGP-IgG). (`rv-celiac-igad-biopsy`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 21 Hit 4:** Hit 4 calls macroenzymes enzyme-autoantibody complexes with an IgG-like half-life of about 3 weeks. (`rv-macroenzyme-composition`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 21 Trap 2:** Trap 2 (and the matching Memory Hook) says citrate plasma gives falsely low CK because chelators strip calcium, the CK cofactor. (`rv-ck-calcium-cofactor`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 78 Hit 2:** Hit 2 and the Do Not Confuse table say 1p/19q codeletion defines oligodendroglioma. (`rv-1p19q-needs-idh`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 78 Trap 4:** Hit 2, Exam Trap 4 and the Do Not Confuse table present 1p/19q codeletion as the predictor of PCV response. (`rv-pcv-not-exclusive`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 13 Hit 4:** Hit 4, Exam Trap 1, the Do-not-confuse table (Table 2) and its closing line call up-coding and unbundling 'illegal fraud' with no qualification. (`rv-upcoding-knowing`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 13 Trap 6:** Trap 6 (and Hit 9) say the lab must give the patient an ABN before the test if Medicare may deny it. (`rv-abn-routine`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 55 Hit 13:** The reviewer says C4d is prominent in 90% of immune-complex GN but negative/trace in 90% of C3 glomerulopathy, calling it a clean discriminator. (`rv-c4d-c3g-discriminator`, needs-verification).
- **Ch. 55 Trap 3:** The reviewer presents PLA2R epitope spreading (CysR to CTLD1/CTLD7) as a predictor of poorer remission without noting that epitope-specific anti-PLA2R assays are not available in clinical practice. (`rv-pla2r-epitope-assay`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 55 Trap 3:** The reviewer's trap ('A single anti-PLA2R level tells you the MN prognosis') implies that one level carries no prognostic information. (`rv-pla2r-baseline-titre`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 67 Hit 4:** Hit 4 (and the Number Vault) presents ~1 SNP per 1000 bp as a fixed genome rate. (`rv-ch67-snp-rate-context`, verified-issue).
- **Ch. 67 Hit 14:** Hit 14 says CFTR mutation analysis expanded cystic fibrosis to milder/atypical forms (CBAVD, chronic pancreatitis, sinopulmonary disease). (`rv-ch67-cftr-related-disorder`, verified-issue).

- **Ch. 45 Hit 15:** Hit 15 gives rare-earth chelate decay as '~50–100 µs' against background '<10 ns'. (`rv-trf-decay-range`, needs-verification).

Automated scan: **23** dated-guideline statements and **134** High-Yield Hits with only/always/never wording. Each must be checked when its lesson is built.

## Imported question bank

The reviewer's Exam Simulator (828 questions, answer key and explanations) is imported as-is at the rights holder's request. This app has **not** verified its questions or answers. A keyword pass links questions to open flags in the accuracy register and shows a note on those questions; 231 questions carry notes so far. A question without a note is not thereby verified. Where a lesson and a simulator explanation disagree, the lesson's checked claims and the register take precedence.

## Open lesson issues

- **ABO Forward and Reverse Typing:** 10 pending claims. These cover infant antibody age and the reverse-typing cutoff, elderly patients, IgM binding sites, tube grading, hemolysis as positive, *Ulex*, rouleaux, A2 subgroups, two ABO determinations, and emergency-release components. There are also 3 open flags: tube-grading method dependence, the age cutoff and specimen type (the discrepancy-classification flag is resolved by the new discrepancy lesson).
- **Spurious CBC Results:** 1 pending claim (rouleaux dispersal; not taught); no flags. Pseudothrombocytopenia prevalence taught as ~0.1% (reviewer: 0.1–2%).
- **Liver Panel Patterns:** 1 pending claim (light-exposure handling / FVII half-life; not taught); no flags. Reviewer quotes an AST:ALT ratio of 3–4:1 for alcoholic hepatitis; the lesson teaches the source-backed >2 threshold.
- **Streptococci and Enterococci ID:** 1 pending claim (bacitracin resistance of group B and enterococci; not taught as a stand-alone fact); 1 register flag (Hit 29). Rapid antigen sensitivity taught as ≈ 85% (≈ 40% with a light inoculum) rather than the reviewer's 31–95% range.
- **Choosing the Graft (HPC sources):** 1 pending claim (HPC ABO expression, plerixafor side effects, cord volume cut-off; not taught); 1 register flag (Hit 12, needs verification). G-CSF taught as 10 µg/kg/day × 4–6 days (reviewer: 5–10 µg/kg × 4–5 days). Bleakley 2022 read as abstract only.
- **Typing von Willebrand Disease:** 1 pending claim (type 1 share, type 3 FVIII level, LD-RIPA concentrations; not taught); 1 register flag (Hit 3). James 2021 read as abstract only; the guideline's cut-offs were taken from Tosetto & Eikenboom 2026.
- **Anion Gap Detective:** 1 pending claim (Winter’s respiratory-alkalosis direction, reviewer’s delta-ratio bands; not taught); 1 register flag (Hit 3). Delta-ratio cut-offs differ between sources (< 1; > 1.2 in one abstract; reviewer > 2.0). The MUDPILES list is not reproduced.
- **Specimen Acceptability and Transport:** 1 pending claim (C. difficile frequency and age criteria, UTI threshold, cotton toxicity; not taught); 1 register flag (Hit 4). IDSA/ASM guide read in its 2013 edition (2018 update abstract only). Urine taught as “4 °C unless ≤ 1 h” (reviewer: 2 h).
- **After the Bag Is Empty (DHTR, lookback):** 1 pending claim (DHTR 2-week presentation, Babesia timing, Philippine lookback rules; not taught); 1 register flag (Hit 14). US federal HIV lookback rules only.
- **Urine Casts (core, Ch. 29):** 1 pending claim (glitter cells, > 30 WBC/hpf, mucus, broad casts; not taught); 2 register flags (Hits 1, 5).
- **Ph-Negative MPNs:** 1 pending claim (NAP in PV, ET > 1000, reactive-thrombocytosis causes; not taught); 2 register flags (Hits 4, 5).
- **Timing the Drug Level (TDM):** 1 pending claim (phenobarbital induction, free-digoxin ultrafiltrate, half-life figures; not taught); no flags. Steady-state percentages taught as arithmetic (1 − 0.5ⁿ).
- **Herpesvirus Timelines (EBV, CMV):** 1 pending claim (EBNA-1 6–12 weeks, heterophile species, < 12-year cut-off; not taught); no flags. Monospot limit taught as “young children” (Baron 2013) rather than the reviewer’s “< 12 years”; Trap 4 not taught.
- **Apheresis Safety (citrate, ASFA, RCE):** 1 pending claim (paresthesia as early sign, calcium dose, circuit targets, RCE hematocrit; not taught); no flags. RCE HbS goal taught as “often 30–50%, varies” (reviewer: < 30%). ASFA tenth edition (2026) read as abstract only.
- **Serous Fluids (core, Ch. 30):** 1 pending claim (SAAG 98% accuracy, IFN-γ cut-off, pH > 7.30; not taught); no flags. Light’s cut-offs taught as “>” (reviewer writes “≥”).
- **Inherited Thrombophilia (Ch. 38):** 1 pending claim (FVL fold-risks, free protein S falling from the 10th week, factor II assays “blind” to G20210A, ACMG/ACOG on MTHFR; not taught); 1 register flag (rv-fvl-risk).
- **Plasma Protein Patterns (Ch. 20):** 1 pending claim (α2-macroglobulin size and 10-fold rise, δ-bilirubin, CRP bacterial vs viral, haptoglobin rebound/hemopexin, ZZ prevalence, myoglobin binding; not taught); 2 register flags (rv-myoglobin-hp, rv-bisalbumin).

- **Testing Cellular Immunity: IGRA (Ch. 46):** 1 pending claim (other cellular assays and figures, anticoagulant rules, CD4 diurnal variation; not taught); no open lesson flags; no register flags. Pai 2014 read via a page-fetch tool (first 100,000 characters).

- **Hypersensitivity Types and Their Lab Footprints (Ch. 44):** 1 pending claim (T-cell subsets, cytokines, therapeutics; not taught); no open lesson flags; no register flags. Teaches the EAACI 2023 expansion (IVa–c, V–VII) alongside the classic four types.
- **Lab Statistics: Reference Intervals and Method Comparison (Ch. 10):** 1 pending claim (other statistics topics, reviewer’s ALT/Method C figures; not taught); no open lesson flags; no register flags. CLSI C28-A3 cited through Ozarda 2016.
- **Automation and the Total Testing Process (Ch. 6):** 1 pending claim (automation definitions, RFP TAT targets, reagent systems, MALDI-TOF figures; not taught); no open lesson flags; no register flags. Plebani’s error distribution cited through Hawkins 2012.
- **Reading Results Like a Detective: Low Anion Gap and Protein Effects (Ch. 9):** 1 pending claim (other Ch. 9 patterns; not taught here); 2 open lesson flags (f-ag-range-ch9, f-ca-ref-ch9) pointing to existing register flags rv-ag-normal and rv-calcium-ref-albumin, which Ch. 9 repeats. Laakman 2022 read as abstract only.
- **Mass Spectrometry: Ionization, MRM and Internal Standards (Ch. 5):** 1 pending claim (instrument details, drug ranges, PKU ratio, ambient MS, scan modes; not taught); no open lesson flags; no register flags. Pitt 2009 read via a page-fetch tool.
- **Testing at the Bedside: Waived Tests, Proficiency Testing and Site Comparisons (Ch. 7):** 1 pending claim (named devices, FDA proposals, POCT connectivity, assay-specific details; not taught); 1 open lesson flag (f-us-only-poct: US CLIA only, Philippine rules not covered); no register flags. Rules checked against eCFR 42 CFR 493.15, 493.35, 493.801 and 493.1281 (2026-10-01 version).
- **Lab Data That Never Forgets: Audit Trails, ACID and NGS Files (Ch. 12):** 2 pending claims (reviewer’s database-design principles: inactivation, status transformation, CRUD/keys; barcode, cognition and LIS-selection details, not taught); 1 open lesson flag (f-us-only-lis: US CLIA record rules only); 1 register flag (rv-acid-isolation). Checked against 42 CFR 493.1291(k) and 493.1105, the PostgreSQL documentation, and the FASTQ (Cock 2010), SAM/BAM (Li 2009) and VCF (Danecek 2011) papers.
- **What a Test Costs: Contribution Margin, Break-Even and NPV (Ch. 13):** 2 pending claims (NPV/IRR ranking rule; reimbursement, coding, leases and budgeting, not taught); 1 open lesson flag (f-cm-definition, contradiction); 2 register flags (rv-contribution-margin, rv-npv-irr). Definitions checked against OpenStax Principles of Accounting, Volume 2 (Managerial Accounting), Sections 3.1, 3.2, 11.2 and 11.4; worked numbers are invented.
- **Research or Quality Work? Belmont Principles and the IRB (Ch. 14):** 2 pending claims (OHRP “intent to publish” guidance, not readable; trial phases, Moore, kickbacks, retention periods, not taught); 1 open lesson flag (f-us-only-ethics); 1 register flag (rv-exempt-determiner). Checked against 45 CFR 46.102, 46.104, 46.107, 46.109, 46.110 and 160.103, Nagai 2022 (Belmont history) and Tobin 2022 (Tuskegee). The Belmont Report and OHRP pages returned HTTP 403 and were not read.
- **C. difficile Testing: GDH, Toxin and NAAT in a Multistep Algorithm (Ch. 57):** all 4 factual claims checked against IDSA/SHEA 2017 (McDonald 2018, read via a page-fetch tool) and Lee 2021; no lesson flags; no register flags. The reviewer’s “~50% of neonates colonized” is taught with the guideline’s wording (“can exceed 40%”).
- **Listeria and Nocardia: Two Gram-Positive Rods That Must Not Be Missed (Ch. 57):** 1 pending claim (“tumbling” motility, cold enrichment, “chalky” colonies, Actinomyces non-acid-fast, TMP-SMX by name: not found in sources read, not taught); no lesson or register flags. Checked against Manyi-Loh 2025, Bongiovanni 2024, Cho 2022 (abstract), Kandi 2015, Saubolle 2003 (read via a page-fetch tool) and Valour 2014.
- **Endocarditis and Blood Cultures: The 2023 Duke-ISCVID Microbiology Rules (Ch. 66, Ch. 57):** 1 pending claim (bone-marrow culture agents; post-dental timing, not taught); 1 open lesson flag (f-hacek-culture-negative, outdated; links register flag rv-hacek-culture). Checked against Fowler 2023 (read via a page-fetch tool), Cuervo 2021 and Shmueli 2020.
- **Gram-Positive Resistance: Penicillinase, mecA/mecC, van Genes and HLAR (Ch. 58):** 2 pending claims (current CLSI M100 reporting text not read, as it is paywalled; the reviewer's vanC infection-control advice is not taught). 4 lesson flags: vanB is not simply "lower-level" (vancomycin MIC 4–1,000 mg/L; teicoplanin susceptibility is what tells it apart); the streptomycin HLAR cutoff depends on method (>1,000 µg/mL broth, >2,000 µg/mL agar); on the Vitek 2 system most mecC MRSA test cefoxitin-resistant but oxacillin-susceptible; nitrocefin is reported less sensitive than the penicillin zone-edge test (needs verification, as the supporting study had only 2 blaZ-positive isolates). 4 register flags proposed (rv-vanb-teicoplanin, rv-hlar-streptomycin-method, rv-oxacillin-s-mecc, rv-mssa-penicillinase-zone-edge). Independent fact-check: all 8 citations re-verified, and overstatements corrected (mecC pattern limited to Vitek 2, a sharp zone edge "suggests" penicillinase, cefoxitin "more reliable than" rather than "best"). Checked against Lakhundi 2018 (Clin Microbiol Rev) and Miller 2014 (Expert Rev Anti Infect Ther), both read via a page-fetch tool for the first part of the article only, plus Paterson 2014, Takayama 2018, O'Driscoll 2015, Shridhar 2019, Padmasini 2014 and Adhikari 2010 (Europe PMC full text).
- **TB Drug Resistance: Xpert MTB/RIF, the 1% Rule and M. bovis (Ch. 59):** 1 pending claim (81-bp rpoB target and hands-on time under 5 min, 28-day susceptibility turnaround target, M. bovis niacin/nitrate reactions). Lesson flags: f-xpert-pooled-accuracy (missing-context: the reviewer gives 69-72% smear-negative sensitivity and, in the Number Vault, 100% specificity; Cochrane pooled figures are 67% (62-72%) and 98%) and f-us-only-tb-dst (guideline-dependent: the RIPE regimen, four-drug panel and rapid-rifampin criteria are US rules). Register flags: rv-xpert-pooled-accuracy, rv-tb-dst-us-guideline. Checked against Horne 2019 (Cochrane), Boehme 2010, Lewinsohn 2017 and Nahid 2016 (ATS/IDSA/CDC; Boehme, Lewinsohn and Nahid read via a page-fetch tool, Lewinsohn as the PMC executive summary, Nahid only the first part of the page), Yusoof 2022, Said 2012, Fang 2025 and Singh 2022. An independent fact-check confirmed every citation's metadata; it corrected the comparison points on transmission, two question rationales, the MDR-contact wording and the 1% rule limitation.
- **Hybridization Stringency, Southern and Northern Blots, and Microarray Labeling (Ch. 68):** 1 pending claim, not taught: the exact 15–45-base oligonucleotide probe length (sources read give about 20–50 nt for RNA FISH probes, or under 50 bp for mismatch discrimination) and "no digestion" for northern blots. Lesson flag f-blots-still-used (missing-context); register flag rv-blots-still-used proposed. Checked against Young 2020 (PeerJ), Hendling 2019, Yilmaz 2012, Gresham 2008, Kubota 2013, Yang 2022, Rossetti 2011, Croning 2010 and Pandelache 2021, all read in Europe PMC full text. No page-fetch tool was used. The NCBI Bookshelf was blocked by a captcha and was not used.
- **Apolipoproteins: apoB, apoC-II/C-III, apoE and Type 3 Dysbetalipoproteinaemia (Ch. 18):** 1 pending claim (the apoB-48 mRNA-editing site from the reviewer memory hook, not taught). 2 open lesson flags: Hit 4 "equal cholesterol and TG elevation" is qualified as missing-context, because the pattern is usually mixed but can lean either way and the standard panel cannot make the diagnosis; Hit 10 "apoC-II deficiency mimics LPL deficiency" is qualified as missing-context, because apoC-II deficiency is usually milder and starts later. 2 proposed register flags (rv-fd-lipid-pattern, rv-apoc2-milder). Penetrance of e2e2 is taught as low and source-dependent (10-15% in Heidemann 2022; under 5% in Pallazola 2020). Checked against open full texts: Behbodikhah 2021, Shapiro 2017, Loving 2020, Kumari 2021, Mahley 2016, Heidemann 2022, Pallazola 2020, Sampson 2022, and Wolska 2017 (read via a page-fetch tool; the first two-thirds of the page was read). The Endotext lipoprotein chapter on NCBI Bookshelf was blocked by a captcha and was not read.
- **Intestinal Protozoa: Entamoeba Look-Alikes, Coccidian Oocysts and GI Panels (Ch. 65):** 1 pending claim (cold Kinyoun / deep fuchsia wording; malaria PCR part of Hit 17); lesson flags f-entamoeba-antigen (missing-context) and f-cystoisospora-size (contradiction); register flags rv-entamoeba-antigen-only and rv-cystoisospora-size proposed. Checked against CDC DPDx pages (Amebiasis, Cryptosporidiosis, Cyclosporiasis, Cystoisosporiasis; fetched directly), Singh 2009, Gerace 2019, Almeria 2019, Casmo 2018 and Tansarli 2025 (Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used). Fact-check pass: Almeria page corrected to E317; Gerace/Casmo size attributions corrected; unsupported 'treat when identified' and 'O&P still has a place' wording removed; GI panel targets qualified as panel-dependent.
- **ESR: The Westergren Method, What Moves It, and Reading the Extremes (Ch. 31):** 1 pending claim (Hit 11's "temporal arteritis/polymyalgia rheumatica typically >90 mm/h", not taught). Lesson flags f-esr-100-cancer (missing-context) and f-esr-gca-90 (uncertain); register flags rv-esr-100-cancer and rv-esr-gca-90 proposed. Checked against Tuzcu 2026, Lorubbio 2024, Amponsah 2026, Markanday 2015, Alende-Castro 2019, Abbag 2007, Manzo 2018 and Sproston 2018, all read as Europe PMC full text. A fact-check pass rechecked every citation and corrected overstated wording. The tilt effect now reads "about 30%" rather than "up to 30%", and it rests on a secondary statement in Amponsah 2026 that cites StatPearls. Sickle cells and spherocytes now "inhibit" rouleaux formation, CRP "falls over about 18-20 h, close to its half-life", and ambient temperature is described as a possible influence only. The ICSH ESR recommendations (1993, 2011, 2017) are not open access and were not read. NCBI Bookshelf (StatPearls) returned a captcha and was not read.
- **Zinc and Copper Labs: Sampling, Zinc-Induced Copper Deficiency and Menkes Disease (Ch. 27):** 1 pending claim for reviewer details that the sources did not support or qualified: Menkes "RBC copper normal"; "dwarfism"; the acrodermatitis enteropathica cut-off of <40 µg/dL (BOND reports a mean of 38 ± 21 µg/dL when signs were present); erythrocyte SOD activity as the better copper marker; and serum zinc running 5–10% higher than plasma. 3 open lesson flags (SOD as copper marker: uncertain; serum vs plasma zinc: missing-context; Menkes neonatal copper values: missing-context). 3 proposed register flags (rv-copper-sod-marker, rv-zinc-serum-plasma, rv-menkes-neonatal-copper). The fact-check corrected four things: Menkes hair timing (hair changes from 1–2 months, diagnosis at 3–6 months); serum vs plasma zinc (the two did not differ when handled alike, rather than 'both valid'); the takeaway, which now uses timing-matched zinc cut-offs instead of fasting morning only; and Menkes described as copper unable to leave gut cells. Sources: the BOND zinc review (King 2015; read via a page-fetch tool, first ~200k of ~344k characters) and Tümer 2010 (read via a page-fetch tool), plus Jaiser 2010, Wahab 2020, Ganaraja 2025, Monangi 2024 and Iskandar 2005 (Europe PMC full text). Two sources were blocked and not read: the NIH ODS copper fact sheet (403) and GeneReviews (captcha).
- **Common Variant Genotyping: Factor V Leiden, HFE C282Y and Why Not MTHFR (Ch. 72):** 1 pending claim: the reviewer's factor V Leiden lifetime penetrance of ~10% was not found in any source read and is not taught. Khan & Dickerman 2006 instead cite an estimate that homozygotes can expect at least one VTE in their lifetime. 2 open lesson flags: f-fvl-ch72-figures (uncertain: prevalence and fold-risk figures differ by study) and f-mthfr-not-thrombophilia (missing-context: calling the association "questionable" understates 2022-2023 guidance that MTHFR variants are not associated with VTE). 2 register flags proposed: rv-fvl-ch72-penetrance and rv-mthfr-not-thrombophilia. Sources: Nakashima & Rogers 2014, Khan & Dickerman 2006, BSH 2022, ASH 2023, Deloughery 2022 and AASLD 2011, all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool was used. The ACG 2019 hemochromatosis guideline was not read because only a one-page summary is open access. An independent fact-check rechecked every citation and corrected 7 places that overstated or misattributed claims (ASH wording, MTHFR rationale and takeaway, mnemonic, H63D wording, claim count).
- **Rapid Identification: Biochemical Systems, MALDI-TOF, Syndromic Panels and 16S (Ch. 57):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's "dozens of targets" per syndromic panel and current panel target counts, not taught); 2 open lesson flags (f-16s-definitive, f-maldi-ast, both missing-context); 2 register flags (rv-16s-definitive, rv-maldi-ast). Checked against Arbefeville 2024, Hou 2019, Calderaro 2024, Torres-Sangiao 2021 and Salimnia 2016 (full text via Europe PMC), and Ramanan 2018, Janda 2007 and Church 2020 (read via a page-fetch tool; for Ramanan and Church only the first 100,000 characters of the page; Church's PMC page first showed a captcha and its ASM page returned 403). An independent fact-check softened turnaround and AST wording and removed an unsourced Shigella clue from Q1.
- **Calling It In: Microbiology Critical Results, Corrections and Early Warning (Ch. 66):** 1 pending claim (chain of custody, collector identity, colony count as a mandatory report element, select-agent/TB reporting specifics, absolute "micro positives never autoverify" wording); lesson flags f-crit-list-local (missing-context: reviewer's fixed critical list taught as examples of lab-defined lists) and f-us-only-reporting (guideline-dependent: US CLIA/state law only, Philippine rules not covered); register flags rv-micro-critical-list-local, rv-micro-reporting-us-law. Checked against 42 CFR 493.1241, 493.1283 and 493.1291 (eCFR 2026-10-01), Lenicek Krleza 2019 (Croatian post-analytics recommendations), Vuljanić 2020, Lam 2016, Buehler 2016, Cantón 2005, Mencacci 2023, Burckhardt 2018 (all Europe PMC full text) and the CDC NNDSS case-surveillance page (fetched directly); no page-fetch tool used. Independent fact-check removed the unsupported "quantity" report element, softened "every plate with growth goes to a technologist" to plates flagged as growth (citing 89% specificity and 38% PPV), and corrected several rationales.
- **Separating Proteins: Size-Exclusion, SDS-PAGE and Isoelectric Focusing (Ch. 4):** 1 pending claim: the lesson does not teach HPLC ~6000 PSI or HbA1c in under 5 min (Hit 18), the IEF 0.02 pH-unit resolution, the electrophoresis voltage and load figures, or OCB pattern types. Two lesson flags are open. f-sds-mass-alone (missing-context): SDS-PAGE separates by mass only if proteins bind a consistent amount of SDS, and membrane, very acidic and glycosylated proteins can run anomalously. f-sec-stokes (missing-context): SEC sorts by Stokes radius, not exact MW, and interactions with the beads can shift elution. Register flags rv-sds-page-mass-anomalies and rv-sec-stokes-radius are proposed. Checked against Hong 2012, Lepedda 2008, Mahmood 2012, Rath 2009 (read via a page-fetch tool), Guan 2015, Frömmel 2018 and Schwenkenbecher 2018. An independent fact-check made these corrections: the 1.4 g/g SDS figure is now taught as the commonly quoted maximum; the reducing-agent statement was narrowed to what the sources say; the PTM effect is now 'pI or Mr'; and one citation's pages were fixed.
- **CSF Oligoclonal Bands: The Five Patterns and Intrathecal IgG (Ch. 47):** 1 pending claim (the Freedman 2005 consensus attribution; the paper is not open access and was not read). 2 lesson flags. f-ocb-ief-method: the reviewer's "IFE" in Hit 11 is ambiguous, because the chapter uses IFE for immunofixation elsewhere, and the sources describe isoelectric focusing followed by an IgG-specific immunoblot or immunofixation on paired CSF and serum. f-ocb-not-ms-specific: types 2 and 3 show intrathecal synthesis but are not specific for MS. Register flags rv-ocb-ief-method and rv-ocb-not-ms-specific proposed. Independent fact-check: fixed the borderline wording (1–2 bands is borderline only where the cut-off is 3, per Hegen 2019), removed an unsupported comparison point and corrected the Schwenkenbecher pages. Checked against Trbojevic-Cepe 2004 (EJIFCC), Jin 2023 (Ann Transl Med), Hegen 2019 (PLoS One), Schwenkenbecher 2018 (Int J Mol Sci) and Rammohan 2009 (Ann Indian Acad Neurol). All five were read as Europe PMC full text, with no page-fetch tool needed.
- **Tumour Genotype and Targeted Drugs: EGFR, RAS and ALK (Ch. 75):** 1 pending claim (FDA anti-EGFR label wording, per-mutation ~45% shares, 10–30% dramatic-response figure, BRAF as anti-EGFR resistance marker); lesson flags: exon 19 del + L858R are ~85–90% not >90% (contradiction), T790M 50–60% depending on assay (method-dependent), BRAF predictive value judged insufficient in 2017 (missing-context), US FDA approvals only (guideline-dependent); register flags rv-egfr-common-share, rv-crc-braf-predictive, rv-ch75-us-approvals. Checked against Ricciuti 2017, Sullivan 2016, Sepulveda 2017 (ASCP/CAP/AMP/ASCO CRC guideline), Golding 2018, Du 2018 and Fisher 2012, all read as Europe PMC full text (no page-fetch tool used); independent fact-check corrected the Sepulveda citation title, softened RAS-mechanism and rationale wording to what the sources state, and added later first-line approvals of ceritinib and alectinib; CAP/IASLC/AMP lung guideline (Lindeman 2013/2018) had no open full text and was not read.
- **B-Cell Neoplasm Markers: t(14;18), IGHV, BRAF V600E and MYD88 (Ch. 77):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's germinal-centre explanation for mutated IGHV, not taught); lesson flags f-ch77-t1418-pcr-coverage (method-dependent), f-ch77-ighv-borderline (missing-context) and f-ch77-myd88-not-exclusive (method-dependent); register flags rv-ch77-t1418-pcr-coverage, rv-ch77-ighv-borderline and rv-ch77-myd88-not-exclusive proposed. Checked against Panjan 2023, Selvi 2012, the ERIC IGHV recommendations (2022 and 2024), the ERIC TP53 recommendations (2018), Falini 2022, Ahmadzadeh 2014, Yan 2024 and Shin 2016 (all Europe PMC full text), plus Tiacci 2011 NEJM (author manuscript read via a page-fetch tool, last part not read; published abstract via Europe PMC). The two versions give slightly different counts (0/193 vs 0/195 other B-cell neoplasms), so the lesson says "every HCL tested, none of nearly 200 others".
- **Reference Intervals, Delta Checks and the Index of Individuality (Ch. 8):** 1 pending claim: the reviewer's HbA1c laboratory range of 4.0–6.0%, which is not taught. 3 lesson flags (f-delta-cause, f-trop-sex, f-hba1c-range), all missing-context. 3 proposed register flags (rv-ch8-delta-check-causes, rv-ch8-troponin-sex-specific, rv-ch8-hba1c-range-overlap). Sources: Ozarda 2016, Lenicek Krleza 2019 (Croatian post-analytical recommendations), Park 2012, Kim 2023 and Chapman 2020 (Europe PMC full text), and the ADA Standards of Care 2026 (read via a page-fetch tool, first part of the page only, Tables 2.1–2.2). CLSI/IFCC C28-A3 itself was not read; its content comes from Ozarda's summary. A second fact-check removed wording the sources did not support: 'recollect', 'best/classic' delta analytes, 'healthy population' in the troponin question, and troponin described as a 'decision line, not the census'.
- **Myositis Autoantibodies (Ch. 53):** 1 pending claim: reviewer prevalence figures for anti-Jo-1, anti-TIF1γ and anti-MDA5; 'nonerosive' arthritis; the epitope-spreading explanation of why IP is hard to interpret. 3 open lesson flags: Hit 7, statin exposure is not required for anti-HMGCR (missing-context); Hit 15, Ro52 plus ARS muscle/joint severity is uncertain, since both sources support worse ILD but neither supports more joint impairment; Hit 9, NT5c1A specificity is method- and comparator-dependent. Register flags rv-hmgcr-statin-naive, rv-ro52-ars-muscle and rv-nt5c1a-specificity proposed. Fact-check fixes: the TIF1γ limitation now says 'many', not 'most', patients have no cancer (PPV 58%); the Ro52 frequency is given per cohort (36% in Yamaguchi 2024 vs two-thirds cited by Palterer 2018); an unsupported 'fast' claim for immunoassays was removed. Checked against Palterer 2018 (Clin Mol Allergy), Mohassel 2018 (J Neuromuscul Dis), Amlani 2019 (Front Immunol), Yamaguchi 2024 (Front Immunol) and Tansley 2020 (Arthritis Res Ther). All were read as full text via Europe PMC (no page-fetch tool used), and their citations were re-verified with meta.sh.
- **Malabsorption Labs: Fecal Elastase-1 and Coeliac Serology with Total IgA (Ch. 23):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's "use human/recombinant tTG" antigen advice; the FE-1 5–6× stool concentration from Memory Hooks); 2 open lesson flags (f-fe1-soft-cutoff: 200 μg/g is widely used but is not an established cut-off, results depend on pretest probability, and watery stool gives falsely low results; f-celiac-igad-biopsy: IgA deficiency affects 2–3% of coeliac patients, a negative IgG test does not exclude the disease, and biopsy is needed when there is malabsorption); register flags rv-fe1-soft-cutoff and rv-celiac-igad-biopsy. Checked against Dominguez-Muñoz 2025 (European PEI guideline), Phillips 2021 (UK PEI guidelines), de la Iglesia 2025 (FE-1 meta-analysis, mostly chronic pancreatitis and cystic fibrosis studies) and Al-Toma 2025 (ESsCD adult coeliac guideline Part 1), all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used. An independent fact-check softened four overstated statements (false-positive certainty, "must repeat", the meta-analysis population).
- **When Enzyme Results Mislead: Macroenzymes, Haemolysis and EDTA (Ch. 21):** 1 pending claim (Oliver–Rosalki eponym; ~3-week IgG-like macroenzyme half-life; citrate/oxalate and calcium as CK cofactor; zinc as ALP cofactor; renin exception; renal failure raising amylase/lipase; bile-salt release of ALP/GGT; GGT induction by ethanol; "unmeasurably low" ALP); 2 lesson flags (f-macroenzyme-composition, missing-context; f-ck-calcium-cofactor, contradiction); 2 register flags proposed (rv-macroenzyme-composition, rv-ck-calcium-cofactor). Checked against Thomas 2002 (EJIFCC), Fujisawa 2023 (Int J Mol Sci), Dedeene 2023 (Biochem Med), Čásenská 2023 (J Clin Lab Anal), Dos Santos 2024 (Korean J Fam Med), Lima-Oliveira 2014 (Biochem Med), Kalas 2021 (World J Hepatol), Radišić Biljak 2025 (Biochem Med; IFCC CK reaction chain) and Leppäniemi 2019 (WSES guidelines, World J Emerg Surg), all read as Europe PMC full text. An independent fact-check removed the unsupported GGT-induction claim, corrected the EDTA study's specimen type (heparin plasma, not serum) and added sources for the CK coupled assay and pancreatitis criteria. The IFCC CK method paper (Hørder 1990, PMC2547813) had no machine-readable full text, the PMC page held only metadata (read via a page-fetch tool), and its PDF download was blocked, so it was not used.
- **Glioma Markers: 1p/19q Codeletion vs MGMT Methylation (Ch. 78):** 1 pending claim, not taught: the Number Vault frequencies (codeletion 50–80%; MGMT 75%/36% for secondary/primary GBM), which use pre-2021 terminology. Lesson flags: f-1p19q-needs-idh (oligodendroglioma needs an IDH mutation plus whole-arm codeletion; FISH can call partial deletions positive) and f-pcv-not-exclusive (the RTOG 9402 benefit came from an unplanned analysis and is "not flawlessly predictive"; PCV is also used for IDH-mutant astrocytoma grade 2). Register flags: rv-1p19q-needs-idh, rv-pcv-not-exclusive. Checked against the Louis 2021 WHO CNS5 summary (read via a page-fetch tool, first 100,000 characters), the Weller 2021 EANO guideline, Cairncross 2013 RTOG 9402 (read via a page-fetch tool), Ball 2020 and Brandner 2021. An independent fact-check changed the Q1 distractors to options the sources support and removed two overstatements.
- **Billing It Right: CPT/HCPCS, Panel Codes, Medical Necessity and the ABN (Ch. 13):** 1 pending claim (Level II "A–V" letter range; PSA codes 84153/G0103 and the PSA-with-heart-failure example, not taught); lesson flags f-upcoding-knowing (missing-context), f-abn-routine (missing-context), f-us-only-coding (guideline-dependent); register flags proposed rv-upcoding-knowing, rv-abn-routine (simulator note for sim-q137). Checked against the CMS HCPCS page, the 2026 CMS NCCI Policy Manual (Ch. I and X), the CMS FFS ABN page and ABN form instructions, the OIG Compliance Program Guidance for Clinical Laboratories (63 FR 45076-45087, 1998), 31 U.S.C. 3729 (via Cornell LII), 42 CFR 410.32, 411.15, 411.404, 411.408 (physician-refund rule, used as a parallel only) and 45 CFR 162.1002 (eCFR 2026-10-01); all read directly with curl/pdftotext, no page-fetch tool used. Independent fact-check re-read every source and tightened six overstated phrasings (specific intent, knowing false claims, genuine doubt, diagnosis vs coverage, other payers).
- **Kidney Immunofluorescence Patterns and Anti-PLA2R Kinetics (Ch. 55):** 1 pending claim group, not taught: intense C1q as the lupus tell, full house unique to lupus, 180 kDa PLA2R, IgAN progression rates, NIH activity/chronicity indices. Lesson flags: f-c4d-c3g (contradiction: Singh 2019 found C4d negative/trace in only 22% of DDD and 64% of C3GN, so C4d does not exclude C3G); f-pla2r-epitope (missing-context: epitope-specific assays are not available in routine practice); f-pla2r-baseline (missing-context: a high baseline anti-PLA2R titre predicts poorer response, so a single level is not uninformative; serial titres are still needed for monitoring). Register flags: rv-c4d-c3g-discriminator, rv-pla2r-epitope-assay, rv-pla2r-baseline-titre. Checked against Akiyama 2019 (F1000Res), Tesar 2021 (Front Immunol), Pickering 2013 C3G consensus (Kidney Int), Singh 2019 and Wang 2020 (Kidney Int Rep), Ye 2023 (Kidney360), Knoppova 2021 and Tota 2023 (J Clin Med). All were read via Europe PMC full text (for Wang 2020, only the abstract and introduction); no page-fetch tool was used. Independent fact-check pass completed.
- **Molecular Results in Context: FISH, Leukaemia Genetics, CFTR and SNPs (Ch. 67):** 1 pending claim (Hit 1 method list, SNP parentage/forensic use, Trap 1 contamination/no-tumour example; not taught); 2 open lesson flags (missing-context: ~1 SNP/1000 bp depends on what is compared; missing-context: single-organ CFTR disease that does not meet CF criteria is a CFTR-related disorder, not CF by genotype alone); register flags proposed rv-ch67-snp-rate-context and rv-ch67-cftr-related-disorder. Fact-check corrected an APL overstatement (ATRA starts on suspicion; FISH gives PML::RARA within hours, per the Brazilian APL consensus 2024) and tightened the CF vs CFTR-related disorder wording (normal sweat chloride in Q3; Farrell cut-offs). Checked against WHO 2022 myeloid (Khoury) and lymphoid (Alaggio) papers, Cui 2016 (FISH), de Figueiredo-Pontes 2024 (APL), CF Foundation consensus Farrell 2008 (read via a page-fetch tool, first ~100,000 of ~122,000 characters; 2017 update not read), Esposito 2020, Bell 2002, Hoehe 2004 and 1000 Genomes 2015.
- **XLA, Hyper-IgM and Reading Vaccine Titres (Ch. 52):** 1 pending claim (hyper-IgE STAT3/DOCK8 and IgE thresholds; age at which isohemagglutinins form); lesson flags f-xla-live-vaccines (guideline-dependent: Hit 4 "NEVER give live vaccines" is broader than the CDC/ACIP table, which lists specific contraindicated live vaccines for severe antibody deficiency and not varicella or rotavirus, although the CDC narrative advises no live or non-live vaccines during immunoglobulin therapy for B-cell deficiency; US-only) and f-ivig-hold (uncertain: Hit 13's fixed 5–6-month IVIG hold is not stated in the sources read; the US practice parameter stops IgG for 4–6 months before re-evaluating stable specific-antibody-deficiency patients, and Hansen 2021 excluded measurements within 6 months of IgG); register flags rv-xla-live-vaccine-list, rv-ivig-hold-interval. Checked against El-Sayed 2019, Cardenas-Morales 2022, Jhamnani 2018, Perez 2017 and Hansen 2021 (Europe PMC full text), plus ICON 2016 (Bonilla) and the CDC Altered Immunocompetence page (both read via a page-fetch tool; ICON only partly).
- **T-Cell Activation and the Lymphocyte Proliferation Assay (Ch. 46):** 1 pending claim (reviewer's <5% of peripheral lymphocytes in S phase, 4–24 h thymidine pulse and 5–10 day antigen culture; no open source read, not taught); lesson flags f-b7-cd86 (missing-context: signal 2 ligands are CD80 and CD86, the B7-1/B7-2 molecules, not CD80 alone) and f-si-formula (method-dependent: one laboratory's SI is background-subtracted ×100, normal ranges are wide and laboratory-specific, and parallel controls were more reliable for that laboratory's <10%-of-normal threshold); register flags rv-ch46-b7-cd86, rv-ch46-si-formula proposed. Checked against Beyersdorf 2015 (Immunotargets Ther), van der Merwe 1997 (J Exp Med), Choi 2025 (Exp Mol Med), Richman 1980 (J Cell Biol), Lee 2022 (Front Pediatr) and Ganesan 2023 (Heliyon), all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used. Independent fact-check corrected unsupported B7 naming (source added), mis-attributed Lee details, an over-general parallel-control claim and an unsupported mnemonic limitation.
- **Homogeneous Immunoassays: EMIT, FPIA and Time-Resolved Fluorescence (Ch. 45):** 1 pending claim (lysozyme as an EMIT label; the reviewer's ">160 kDa" bound FPIA complex; the reviewer's "~50–100 µs" vs "<10 ns" decay figures, none of them taught); 2 open lesson flags (f-fpia-complex-size uncertain, f-trf-decay-range missing-context); 2 proposed register flags (rv-fpia-complex-size, rv-trf-decay-range). Checked against Darwish 2006, Sanavio 2015, Maragos 2009, Mukhametova 2026, Hagan 2011 and Qriouet 2019, all read as full text through Europe PMC (no page-fetch tool used). An independent fact-check softened three overstatements (low-level protein "better served by" a wash format, confirmation "by an independent method such as chromatography", calibration-curve wording) and fixed two objects that ran off the canvas.
- **Lymphocyte Subsets: CD4:CD8 Ratio, Th1/Th2, Tregs and NK Cells (Ch. 44):** 1 pending claim (Th1 driving delayed-type hypersensitivity, Hit 4; not taught because no source was read for it); 1 open lesson flag (f-cd4cd8-ratio-range, missing-context: the fixed 2:1 ratio is really a range of about 1.5 to 2.5 that depends on the population, and in HIV the ratio falls partly because CD8 cells expand); 1 proposed register flag (rv-cd4cd8-ratio-range). Checked against McBride & Striker 2017 (PLoS Pathog), Choi 2014 (Immune Netw), Luckheeram 2012 and Cools 2007 (Clin Dev Immunol), Barzaghi & Passerini 2021 (Front Pediatr), Abel 2018 (Front Immunol), Chen 2024 (Signal Transduct Target Ther) and Jing 2015 (PLoS One), all read as Europe PMC full text. An independent fact-check confirmed every citation and softened five overstated cues (reference values are population-specific, the ratio is associated with immune ageing rather than marking it, IPEX Treg-like cells suppress poorly rather than not at all, NK cells target cells with absent or reduced MHC class I). No page-fetch tool was used.
- **Glucose at 340 nm vs the Bedside Meter (Ch. 28):** 1 pending claim (NADPH at 340 nm, 340 nm-decrease assays, lactate/TG/CK variants, tetrazolium/formazan, calibrating personal meters against lab values); 3 open lesson flags (oxygen interference is glucose oxidase-specific; acetaminophen CGM interference is system-dependent; reviewer's haematocrit mechanism is simplified); 3 proposed register flags (rv-ch28-poct-oxygen-enzyme, rv-ch28-cgm-acetaminophen, rv-ch28-hct-mechanism). Checked against Wolfe 2024 (Molecules), Lacorn 2025 (J AOAC Int), Finfer 2013 (Crit Care), Ashar 2026 (EJIFCC), Calhoun 2018 (J Diabetes Sci Technol), and Erbach 2016 and Ginsberg 2009 (J Diabetes Sci Technol; both read via a page-fetch tool). An independent fact-check qualified the claims on the reference method, haematocrit correction, icodextrin (affects both strip types) and monitoring vs diagnosis. The StatPearls Hexokinase Method page was blocked (NCBI browser check; Europe PMC 403) and was not read.
- **Reading Coagulation Patterns: Heparin, INR, Mixing Studies and DIC (Ch. 9):** 1 pending claim group (INR >4 means reduce or stop warfarin; warfarin does not prolong the aPTT; D-dimer largely replaced FSP); 3 open lesson flags (f-inr-valve-ch9 outdated, f-dic-ddimer-ch9 missing-context, f-mix-incubation-ch9 missing-context); 3 proposed register flags (rv-inr-valve-ch9, rv-dic-ddimer-ch9, rv-mix-incubation-ch9). Checked against Barouqa 2026, van der Helm 2026, Roskell 2013, Langenaeken 2023, Johansson 2024, Santoro 2023, Iba 2023, Toh 2016 and Wada 2014 (Europe PMC full text). Independent fact-check: corrected the van der Helm title, qualified the lupus anticoagulant and FVIII-inhibitor mixing behaviour to Santoro's Table 3, sourced the ISTH fibrinogen threshold (<1 g/L) to Toh 2016, and changed 'overt DIC' to 'compatible with overt DIC'. The 2021 ESC/EACTS valve guideline (PMC9725093) was fetched with a page-fetch tool, but its INR target table could not be read, so it is not cited.
- **Parentage Statistics: Paternity Index, Exclusion Probability and W (Ch. 74):** 3 pending claims (AABB Standards "PI ≥ 100" wording, not read; "≥2 independent systems" exclusion rule, not taught; "CPE ≥ 0.995 / >99.9% falsely accused excluded", no source); lesson flags f-pi100-odds (contradiction: PI 100 at a 0.5 prior means 100:1 odds, about 99.0%, not 99:1), f-pi-threshold (guideline-dependent: in the 2013 AABB annual report 65% of accredited labs used LR 100 for trios, and other thresholds such as 1,000 may be used depending on the relationship; China uses CPI ≥ 10,000; US/AABB only, Philippine rules not covered), f-two-exclusions (missing-context: 1–2 mismatches may be mutations, one lab required ≥4 excluding loci); register flags rv-ch74-pi100-odds, rv-ch74-pi-threshold, rv-ch74-two-exclusions. Checked against Thompson 2013 (Front Genet), Thelingwani 2024 (Sci Rep), Ge & Budowle 2020 (PLoS Genet), Gao 2019 (PLoS One), De Kock 2021 (Afr Health Sci), Vandeputte 2012 (Genet Sel Evol), Aşicioglu 2004 (Dis Markers), Lee 2013 (Croat Med J), all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used; AABB Standards text not openly available and not read. Independent fact-check: citations matched; corrected the overstatement that the other trio labs "used 1000".
- **Forty-Five Chromosomes: Monosomy X, Turner Karyotypes and Robertsonian Carriers (Ch. 71):** 0 pending claims (6 checked, 2 analogy); 3 open lesson flags (Turner karyotype percentages contradict the 2024 guideline; Y-material/gonadectomy missing context; der(21;21) "100%" lacks trisomy rescue); 3 proposed register flags (rv-turner-karyotype-mix, rv-turner-y-gonadectomy, rv-rob2121-rescue). Checked against Gravholt 2024 (Eur J Endocrinol Turner guideline), Yang 2017, Cheng 2025, Jackson 2018 and Zhao 2015, all read as full text via Europe PMC (no page-fetch tool used). An independent fact-check confirmed all citation metadata. It qualified the autosomal-monosomy mechanism ("likely"), replaced the reason given for counting 30 metaphases with the guideline's (detects about 10% mosaicism), softened the meiotic-risk wording, and dropped an unsupported full ISCN string for der(21;21).
- **Expression Arrays: Normalization Assumptions, Degraded RNA and the mRNA–Protein Gap (Ch. 70):** 1 pending claim (nylon macroarray autofluorescence and the two-colour ratiometric limitation, Hit 9 and Trap 1, not taught because no readable open source was found); 1 lesson flag (missing-context: Hit 15's 1536-target DASL limit applies to the original assay, and April 2009 describes a whole-genome DASL covering about 24,000 RefSeq genes); 1 register flag proposed (rv-dasl-whole-genome). Checked against Hicks & Irizarry 2015 (Genome Biol), Zhao 2020 (Sci Rep), Park 2003 (BMC Bioinformatics), April 2009 (PLoS One) and Vogel 2010 (Mol Syst Biol), all read as Europe PMC full text with no page-fetch tool used. A fact-check pass corrected one citation (Vogel author spelling) and toned down four overstated statements: the mRNA–protein share, the RNA-input limitation, the FFPE 'resource' wording, and 'never proves' in the takeaway.
- **TB Infection Testing: Mantoux Cutoffs, IGRA and BCG (Ch. 59):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer calls BCG "attenuated" M. bovis; the pages read do not say this, so the lesson says only "derived from M. bovis"). 3 open lesson flags: the reviewer's TST cutoff groups are shorter than CDC's (missing-context); M. flavescens is missing from the NTM that share the IGRA antigens (missing-context); the TST and BCG rules are US-only, while the 2011 BCG World Atlas lists the Philippines with a birth-dose BCG policy (guideline-dependent). 3 proposed register flags: rv-tst-cutoff-groups, rv-igra-ntm-flavescens, rv-tst-bcg-us-guideline. The lesson also qualifies NTM cross-reaction: Pai 2014 calls NTM a clinically unimportant cause of false-positive TSTs outside specific settings. Checked against Pai 2014 Clin Microbiol Rev, the ATS/IDSA/CDC guideline (Lewinsohn 2017 Clin Infect Dis), the CDC tuberculin skin test and BCG pages and the WHO BCG page (all read via a page-fetch tool), plus Zwerling 2011 PLoS Med (BCG World Atlas, Europe PMC full text).
- **Direct Smears, Sterile Fluids and Quantitative Respiratory Cultures (Ch. 66):** 1 pending claim (blood, synovial fluid, deep tissue and bone as normally sterile sites; any organism from a sterile site as possibly significant; thick, wet or unfixed smear as the commonest cause of an unreadable Gram stain; Hit 37 VP-shunt organisms and multiplex panels not taught); lesson flags f-vap-semiquant (missing-context), f-ao-sensitivity (uncertain), f-16s-contamination (missing-context); register flags rv-vap-semiquant, rv-ao-sensitivity, rv-16s-sterile-contamination proposed. Checked against Miller 2018 IDSA/ASM guide, Kalil 2016 IDSA/ATS HAP/VAP guideline and Tunkel 2017 IDSA ventriculitis guideline (all three read via a page-fetch tool, partial sections; direct NCBI access returned a captcha), Sharma 2020, Yoo 2020, Sautter 2024 and Vijayakumar 2023 (Europe PMC full text). Fact-check pass removed blood from the taught sterile-fluid list (unsupported by sources read) and softened contaminant and oral-flora wording.
- **Wound Culture, Pseudomonas Clues and the AmpC Trap (Ch. 57):** 1 pending claim (sneaker-insole Pseudomonas reservoir, aspirates as a specimen, and the site-by-site pathogen map of Hit 42); lesson flags f-space-blanket (contradiction: Hit 12's blanket SPACE rule), f-swab-never (method-dependent: Hit 42's absolute "never a surface swab") and f-puncture-two (missing-context: Hit 26 names only P. aeruginosa); register flags rv-space-ampc-blanket, rv-wound-swab-levine, rv-puncture-two-organisms. Independent re-check corrected the cephalosporin-emergence range from 3–19% to 3–21%, narrowed the "other SPACE organisms" teaching (C. freundii is a high expresser; Pseudomonas lies outside the primer's treatment advice), removed unsupported aspirate and foot-specific wording, and rewrote Q2/Q3. Checked against Tamma 2019 AmpC primer (PMC6763639, read via a page-fetch tool), Nelson 2018 CODIFI (PMC5879729, Europe PMC full text), Behzadi 2021 (PMC7823828, Europe PMC full text), Pachori 2019 (PMC6545445, Europe PMC full text) and Thakolkaran 2019 (PMC6584206, read via a page-fetch tool).
- **HLA Typing Resolution and Donor Matching (Ch. 50):** 1 pending claim (reviewer's 5-year graft-survival figures, "single HLA-C mismatches hurt engraftment", "HLA-A mismatches have the largest impact", SSP as the deceased-donor method). Lesson flags f-hct-locus-impact (contradiction) and f-dp-serology (outdated); register flags rv-hct-locus-impact and rv-dp-serology proposed. Checked against the Dehn 2019 NMDP/CIBMTR guideline (Blood), the Marsh 2010 and Marsh 2026 WHO HLA nomenclature reports, Geo 2024 (Med Princ Pract) and Gramlick 2022 (Transplant Direct), all read in full text via Europe PMC (no page-fetch tool used). A fact-check pass toned down several statements: NGS phasing, the serology description, the 7/8 hook answer (now conditional on an ARD difference) and the takeaway (8/8 is the guideline's aim).
- **Heparin-Induced Thrombocytopenia: 4Ts, PF4 Assays and the Switch (Ch. 39):** 1 pending claim (Hit 5 heparin neutralisation to isolate the warfarin effect; Hit 11 EIA excess-heparin confirmatory step and exact 98.1/89.4% figures; Hit 12 heparin resistance >35,000 U/day); lesson flags f-4t-low-points (contradiction), f-eia-specificity (method-dependent), f-fondaparinux (missing-context); register flags rv-hit-4t-low-points, rv-hit-eia-specificity, rv-hit-fondaparinux-context proposed. Checked against the ASH 2018 HIT guideline (Cuker 2018; read via a page-fetch tool, partially), Warkentin and Greinacher 2023, Marchetti 2021 and Patriarcheas 2020; an independent fact-check pass softened the platelet-transfusion and fondaparinux wording, fixed an inconsistent 4Ts question stem and added source support for flush HIT and assay specificity.
- **B-Cell Clones on Flow: Restriction, MBL, CLL and Hematogones (Ch. 35):** 1 pending claim (reactive clonal T-cell expansions from Trap 3, not taught); 2 open lesson flags (Hit 14 "never both" and "restriction proves clonality", qualified by kappa+/lambda+ normal fetal B cells and by lambda-restricted hematogones without molecular clonality; Hit 6 lacks WHO-HAEM5's "no other features of CLL/SLL" condition and the low-count vs CLL-type MBL split, and wrongly applies the 5,000/μL requirement to SLL); 2 proposed register flags (rv-ch35-kappa-lambda-dual, rv-ch35-mbl-other-features). Checked against WHO-HAEM5 lymphoid (Alaggio 2022), ERIC/ESCCA CLL harmonisation (Rawstron 2018), Gajzer & Fromm 2025, Galigalidou 2021, FILO recommendations (Quinquenel 2020), Brazilian CLL guideline (Chiattone 2025), Singh 2023, Carulli 2014, Guillory 2016 (all Europe PMC full text) and Pauza 1993 (abstract only).
- **Counting Blasts: The MDS–AML Line and the APL Emergency (Ch. 34):** 1 pending claim (500-cell differential, inv(16) notation, reviewer's older Hb <10 / platelets <100 cytopenia thresholds); 3 lesson flags (MDS cytopenia thresholds outdated, EB1/EB2 and ring-sideroblast rule outdated, genetic AML blast cutoff guideline-dependent); register flags rv-mds-cytopenia-2022, rv-mds-eb-ib-terminology and rv-aml-genetic-cutoff-ch34 proposed. Checked against Khoury 2022 (WHO-HAEM5), Gurnari 2021 and Yilmaz 2021 (full text via Europe PMC), and against Arber 2022 (ICC) and Sanz 2019 (ELN APL), both read via a page-fetch tool. Fact-check fixes: marrow 3 is now taught as 'suspected APL, emergency treatment while PML::RARA is confirmed' instead of 'APL whatever the count'; the haemorrhage rationale now follows the ELN wording; the ICC comparison is qualified; prerequisites now point to related concepts (ch34.figure, ch35.hit1).
- **Thyroid Function Patterns: TSH First, T3 Toxicosis, Graves and Iodine (Ch. 25):** 0 pending claims (5 checked). Lesson flags: f-jod-basedow-load (Hit 11 "iodine deficiency → Jod-Basedow": the trigger is an iodine load and deficiency is a risk factor; the Wolff–Chaikoff block is transient and normal glands escape), f-trab-sensitivity (Hit 9 "TRAb ~85%" vs ~97–98% for current assays), f-tsh-alone (Hit 3 leaves out when TSH alone misleads). Register flags: rv-jod-basedow-iodine-load, rv-trab-sensitivity, rv-tsh-alone-limits. Checked against Koulouri 2013 and Sriphrapradang and Bhasipol 2016 (T3-toxicosis definition), both read in full text, and against the Kahaly 2018 ETA guideline, Lee and Pearce 2023, Leung and Braverman 2014 and Klubo-Gwiezdzinska 2022, all four read via a page-fetch tool. The ATA 2016 guideline was not read. StatPearls was blocked by a captcha.
- **New Reagent Lots, Commutability and PT Peer Grading (Ch. 11):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's absolute rule never to change the SD, its Deming example of slope 1.00 and intercept -3 mg/dL, the 1483 QC-lot combinations, the duPont Dimension cholesterol example, and the 0.6% inlier probability at 2.5 SDI); 2 lesson flags (f-sdi-threshold method-dependent, f-lot-sd missing-context); register flags rv-pt-sdi-warning-zone and rv-lot-change-qc-sd. Teaches |SDI| up to 2 as acceptable, 2-3 as a warning and 3 or more as unsatisfactory (ISO/IEC 17043 convention; scheme-dependent, KEQAS uses > 3). Teaches resetting QC targets after patient samples agree, with Thompson and Chesher's caveat that a large QC shift with no patient change calls for checking the comparison's statistical power. Checked against Thompson and Chesher 2018 (read via a page-fetch tool because Europe PMC returned no full text), Chen 2019, Gruber 2024, Gosselin 2024 (ICSH), Kristensen and Meijer 2017, Coucke and Soumali 2017, Kim 2021 (KEQAS), Ricos 2022 and Vierbaum 2024; independently fact-checked, with citations re-verified against Europe PMC.
- **Alcohol, Antidepressant and Lithium Genetics (Ch. 73):** 1 pending claim, not taught: that lithium is the only mood stabilizer thoroughly examined pharmacogenomically. 3 open lesson flags:
- **Making and Firing IgE: Class Switching, Hyper-IgE and Mast-Cell Mediators (Ch. 56):** 1 pending claim (reviewer details not taught: mucosal mast-cell IL-5/C5a traits, histamine pg/cell and 10x basophil content, FcεRI receptor numbers, exact "up to 1000x" potency, HIES upper bound 50,000 kU/L); 3 lesson flags (f-hies-ige-range, f-pgd2-cox, f-cyslt-lte4; all missing-context, open); register flags proposed rv-hies-ige-range, rv-pgd2-cox-isoform and rv-cyslt-lte4-potency. Checked against Galletta 2024 (Life Basel), Durandy & Kracker 2012 (Arthritis Res Ther), Nagata & Suzuki 2022 (Cells), Krystel-Whittemore 2015 (Front Immunol), Komi 2020 (Clin Rev Allergy Immunol), Kanaoka & Boyce 2014 (AAIR), Rittchen & Heinemann 2019 (Cells), Domingo 2018 (Respir Res), Beck 2019 (Front Immunol) and Ansotegui 2020 (WAO J). All full texts were read through the Europe PMC REST API; no page-fetch tool was used. An independent fact-check pass limited leukotriene potency to inhaled LTC4/LTD4, softened overstated wording (IL-21, cross-linking, mediator sequence) and added an open source for the MCT/MCTC names.
- **Heme In, Pigment Out: ALA Synthase, Porphyria Testing and Urobilinogen (Ch. 32):** 1 pending claim (antibiotics giving falsely low fecal urobilinogen in patients, the Number Vault 80–90% fecal urobilinogen figure, lead's enzyme targets; none taught); 3 open lesson flags (the Ehrlich/Watson-Schwartz screen needs quantitative urine PBG/ALA follow-up, \"free\" erythrocyte protoporphyrin should be zinc protoporphyrin, and the antibiotic half of Trap 2 is unverified); 3 register flags proposed (rv-ch32-pbg-quantitative, rv-ch32-zinc-protoporphyrin, rv-ch32-urobilinogen-antibiotics). Checked against Bissell and Wang 2015, Wang 2023 (AGA expert review), Aarsand 2025, Hennig 2016, Kipp 2025, Hall 2024, Su 2024, and McMaster and Elman 1925 (abstract only: the full text is offered only as a PDF, which was not read). All other sources were read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool was used. An independent fact-check corrected the takeaway: urine PBG detects an attack, and genetic testing confirms the AIP type. It also corrected the 5-fold wording (this is the level during an attack, not a diagnostic cut-off), the deconjugation step (human or bacterial beta-glucuronidases) and the stool-pigment wording.
- **Prenatal Serum Screening: Maternal AFP, Neural Tube Defects and the Down Syndrome Screen (Ch. 26):** 1 pending claim, none of it taught: Trap 4's race (~10–15%) and diabetes (~20%) MSAFP adjustment sizes, Hit 7's 12–14-week peak with a ~10%/week fall, and Hit 6's first-trimester serum-only 82–87% and integrated ~98% at <0.5% false positive. Lesson flags: f-down-detection (contradiction), f-ama-35 (missing-context), f-msafp-timing (uncertain), f-msafp-vs-us (guideline-dependent). Register flags proposed: rv-down-screen-detection, rv-down-ama-35, rv-msafp-peak-timing, rv-msafp-vs-ultrasound. Checked against ACMG 2009 (Driscoll and Gross, Genet Med), Kim 2023 (Obstet Gynecol Sci), Salih 2014 (Saudi Med J), Głowska-Ciemny 2024 (J Clin Med) and Czeizel 2013 (Nutrients). All five full texts were read via Europe PMC; no page-fetch tool was used. An independent fact-check pass corrected the Driscoll author string and limited the 5% screen-positive qualifier to the figures ACMG states it for. It also changed the quad screen to ~75% (<35) / >80% (≥35), softened 'many guidelines' to 'some', and attributed ultrasound-first NTD screening as Kim 2023 does (JOGC/SOGC primary, ACOG PB 187). ACOG Practice Bulletin 187 and SOGC guideline 410 were not open access, were not read, and are cited only as Kim 2023 summarises them.
- **MGUS, Smoldering Myeloma and Multiple Myeloma: The IMWG Fences (Ch. 47):** 2 pending claims (the reviewer's IgG ≥2 g/dL / IgA ≥1 g/dL "suspicious for malignancy" thresholds; the plasmacytoma route to myeloma and sFLC monitoring of light-chain escape). Lesson flags: f-mgus-prevalence (contradiction: ~3% at ≥50 and ~5% at ≥70, not ~3% at ≥70), f-mgus-followup (guideline-dependent: IMWG, EMN and UK/Nordic schedules differ), f-mgus-mspike-threshold (uncertain), f-mgus-smm-fence (missing-context: MGUS also needs M-protein <3 g/dL, and SMM can be met by M-protein ≥3 g/dL alone). Register flags proposed: rv-mgus-prevalence-age, rv-mgus-followup-interval, rv-mgus-mspike-threshold, rv-mgus-smm-mprotein-fence. Fact-check pass: the CRAB definitions now include the ULN/LLN parts, and an unsupported 'usually' was removed. Checked against Abeykoon 2022 (Fac Rev), Lakshman 2018 and Mateos 2020 (Blood Cancer J), and the IMWG Mass Spectrometry Committee report (Murray 2021, Blood Cancer J), all read in full text via Europe PMC with no page-fetch tool. The IMWG 2014 Lancet Oncology paper is not open access and was not read, so its criteria are taught as the open sources summarise them.
- **Electrodes and Apertures: ISE, pCO₂ Electrode, Coulter Counting and Coulometric Chloride (Ch. 4):** 1 pending claim (RBC >36 fL and WBC >35 fL impedance thresholds, time-based coulometric calculation, Faraday arithmetic; none taught). Lesson flags: f-coulter-size-windows (method-dependent: the 2–20 fL platelet window is documented for one Beckman Coulter analyser only, and brands differ) and f-ise-activity-indirect (missing-context: ISEs sense activity in serum water, and indirect ISE gives pseudohyponatremia when lipid or protein is high). Register flags proposed: rv-ch4-coulter-size-windows and rv-ch4-ise-indirect. Checked against Wang 2008, Wardak 2023, Dukić 2016, Umeda 2021, Aziz 2023, Bryan 2012, Baccini 2020, Saran 2022, Jose 2025, Marvelli 2020 and Pagaduan 2018, all read as Europe PMC full text, and Taylor 1988 (abstract only; the full text is available only as a PDF). The CFF 2017 and CLSI sweat-testing guidelines were not read directly because they are not open access. An independent fact-check corrected 'E ∝ log activity' to 'E tracks log activity' and removed one unsupported statement about the coulometric endpoint.
- **Exome Yield, mtDNA Heteroplasmy, Secondary Findings and Testing Children (Ch. 72):** 2 pending claims, neither taught: the reviewer's "genuine psychosocial harm" and "<18" cutoff for testing children, and "VUS results are a major counseling challenge". Lesson flags: f-acmg-sf-list-current (outdated), f-predictive-testing-children (missing-context), f-exome-size-variant-count (method-dependent), f-genomic-guidance-us-only (guideline-dependent). Register flags proposed: rv-acmg-sf-list-current, rv-predictive-testing-children-rationale, rv-exome-size-variant-count, rv-genomic-guidance-us-only. Sources: Govers 2021, Stitziel 2011 and Clark 2018 (Europe PMC full text). Five more were read via a page-fetch tool because Europe PMC full text was empty: Parikh 2015 (Mitochondrial Medicine Society), Botkin 2015 (ASHG), and Green 2013, Miller 2021 and Lee 2025 (ACMG). For Parikh and Botkin, only the first ~100,000 characters were read. Independent fact-check: VUS wording narrowed to "not returned as secondary findings", tissue wording aligned to "muscle or liver", and the unquantified "many mitochondrial diseases are nuclear" changed to "can be nuclear".
