- **POCT Lockouts, Traceability and Connectivity (Ch. 7):** 1 pending claim (the POCT01-A2 "device interface" and "observation reporting interface" names; the central-lab volume and iatrogenic anaemia argument for inpatient glucose testing; annual procedure review signed by the director "or designee"); 2 open lesson flags (POCT01-A2 archived, missing-context; CLIA procedure sign-off, guideline-dependent, US-only); register flags rv-ch7-poct01-archived, rv-ch7-procedure-signoff. Checked against Fung 2020 (Pract Lab Med), Shaw 2016 (Pract Lab Med), Khan 2019 (EJIFCC), Larsson 2015 (Ups J Med Sci), Erasmus 2021 (EJIFCC), the CLSI POCT01 catalogue page (read via a page-fetch tool) and 42 CFR 493.1251/493.1283 (eCFR 2026-10-01). An independent fact-check replaced one unsupported distractor (autoverification), rewrote two rationales to match the sources, presented the 3–5% transcription error rate as reported for inpatient ward settings, and qualified the takeaway so it no longer says all results pass through a data manager.
- **Open Reagents, Middleware and Local Turnaround Goals (Ch. 6):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's example RFP TAT maximums of 30/45/20/30 min, workcell definitions, master lots, tube sampling/carryover, MALDI-TOF figures, immunoassay throughput, TLA planning sequence; none taught as fact). Lesson flags: f-ch6-las-mw-overlap (missing-context), f-ch6-open-validation (missing-context), f-ch6-us-only (guideline-dependent; Philippine rules not covered). Register flags proposed: rv-ch6-middleware-las-overlap, rv-ch6-open-system-validation. Checked against Armbruster 2014 (Clin Biochem Rev; full text read from the PMC article page because the Europe PMC full-text endpoint returned no text; authors are Abbott employees), Nam & Park 2025 (Ann Lab Med), Krasowski 2014 (J Pathol Inform), Hawkins 2007 (Clin Biochem Rev; full text read from the PMC article page) and 42 CFR 493.1253 (eCFR 2026-10-01). Independent fact-check pass: TAT-goal wording softened to 'no agreed universal goal', an unsupported cue and the misread Krasowski figures removed, and one Q3 distractor rewritten.
- **MHC Class III: C4A/C4B, C2 and Factor B Deficiency (Ch. 51):** 1 pending claim (C4B 5–10× haemolytic activity, 35% with one C4 null allele, C4B*Q0 3.5× meningitis risk, C4A*Q0 and SLE, SC01/≥3-Mb extended haplotype, HSP70 in class III, "no homozygous factor B deficiency", C2 as the most common complement deficiency, which Macedo 2016 supports only for one ESID series of 77 patients); lesson flags f-factor-b-lethal (contradiction), f-c4-copy-number (uncertain), f-c2-figures (uncertain), f-complotype-scope (missing-context); register flags rv-factor-b-not-lethal, rv-c4-copy-number, rv-c2-deficiency-figures, rv-complotype-scope. Checked against Deakin 2006 (BMC Genomics), Arnaiz-Villena 2023 (Sci Rep), Wang 2021 (Front Immunol), Macedo 2016 (Front Immunol), El Sissy 2019 (Front Immunol), Bougeard 2026 (Eur J Immunol) and Harris 2012 (Trends Immunol), all read as Europe PMC full text (no page-fetch tool used). Independent fact-check corrected: unsupported alpha-chain and blood-group-residue wording, overstated factor B infection uniformity, a missing C4 in the classical-pathway CH50 pattern, and the takeaway now says "classical-pathway gap such as C2".
- **Urine Crystals, Stones and Yeast (Ch. 29):** 1 pending claim (budding as the yeast-versus-RBC separator, 'bacteria are far smaller', stone-type percentages); 3 open lesson flags (staghorn stones not always struvite, oxalate crystals often absent in ethylene glycol poisoning, sediment yeast may be a contaminant); 3 register flags proposed (rv-staghorn-not-always-struvite, rv-ethylene-glycol-crystals-absent, rv-urine-yeast-contaminant). Checked against Lee 2022, Gaggar 2024 (its Table 3 appearance column looks offset, so crystal shapes were cross-checked with Lee 2022), Torricelli 2020, Diri 2018, Kowalczyk 2021, Abou-Elela 2017, Latus 2012, Poloni 2020 and Delanghe 2014, all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used. Independent fact-check corrected the citation metadata (Gaggar DOI, Poloni E245) and softened wording that went beyond the sources (crystal formation, urease 'not all' Klebsiella/Staphylococcus, glycolate metabolism, 'struvite can signal').
- **Cirrhosis Scores and AFP: Child-Pugh, MELD, the INR Caveat and HCC (Ch. 22):** 1 pending claim (Child-Pugh 2-year survival 85/57/35%, MELD 3-month mortality bands and TIPS cut-offs, low-ISI reagent direction, HCV-to-cirrhosis-to-HCC percentages, HCC essentially only in cirrhosis). Lesson flags: f-meld-version (outdated: US allocation moved to MELD-Na in 2016 and MELD 3.0 in 2023; the lesson teaches the lab-only MELD without the original cause-of-cirrhosis term), f-meld-us-only (guideline-dependent: US OPTN policy only, Philippine rules not covered), f-cp-survival (uncertain), f-afp-diagnosis (outdated: AASLD 2023 no longer uses AFP to diagnose HCC; a new lesion of 1 cm or more goes to multiphase CT or MRI), f-afp-cutoff (guideline-dependent: AASLD 20 ng/mL vs APASL 200 ng/mL). Register flags: rv-meld-version-allocation, rv-child-pugh-survival, rv-afp-400-diagnosis, rv-afp-surveillance-cutoff. Checked against Guo 2025, Pinter 2016, Endale Simegn 2022, Peng 2016, Laubham 2018, Singal 2023 (AASLD HCC guidance; read via a page-fetch tool, first ~100,000 characters only), Zhang 2020 and Omata 2017 (APASL). An NCBI Bookshelf Child-Pugh page was blocked by a browser check and was not read. Independent fact-check pass corrected the 'original MELD' labelling, removed unsourced 'warfarin' and physiology wording, and aligned the next-step answer with AASLD's multiphase CT/MRI instruction.
- **Pseudohyperkalaemia and Glucose-Corrected Sodium (Ch. 15):** all 7 factual claims checked, none pending (source-checked). Lesson flags: f-na-factor (method-dependent: the 1.6 vs 2.4 correction factor, with a larger shift above 400 mg/dL), f-pseudok-consequence (missing-context: the same artifacts can mask true hypokalaemia) and f-cold-storage (missing-context: the reviewer lists cold agglutinins, which have only case-report support, but leaves out cold storage temperature). Register flags: rv-glucose-na-factor, rv-pseudok-masks-hypok, rv-pseudok-cold-storage. Sources: Asirvatham 2013, Fujimaru 2025, Umińska 2026, Varga 2019, Baldrighi 2018, Lee 2022 (Korean Society of Nephrology), Chuang 2020 and Mody 2026 (case abstract), all read as Europe PMC full text without a page-fetch tool. The KDIGO 2020 conference report the reviewer cites is not open access and was not read. An independent fact-check pass corrected the cold-agglutinin claim, the hook's 'normal either way' answer and several overstated phrasings.
- **P Values, Type I and II Errors, and the Multiple-Testing Trap (Ch. 10):** 1 pending claim (reviewer's "highly significant: p < 0.001" label and "journals want two-sided tests", not taught); 2 open lesson flags (Hit 6/Trap 2 "always" forced by inflating n needs the non-zero-effect qualifier; Hit 7/Trap 3 Bonferroni presented without its conservative/Type II caveat); register flags rv-large-n-significance, rv-bonferroni-conservative. Checked against Greenland 2016, Banerjee 2009, Sullivan and Feinn 2012 (read via a page-fetch tool), Ranganathan 2016 and Andrade 2019; the reviewer's own mnemonic was replaced with an original memory aid.
- **Oncogenes Stuck ON: Receptor Activation, HER2, MYC and ctDNA (Ch. 76):** 1 pending claim group (c-myc "75-kDa", anti–c-myc antibody rates, ctDNA "1 in 10,000" limit, HER2 "25–50% at 40- to 190-fold", <15 ng/mL survival claim; none taught); lesson flags f-ctdna-pancreas (contradiction), f-her2-serum (missing-context), f-receptor-routes (missing-context); register flags rv-ctdna-pancreas-sens, rv-her2-serum-ecd, rv-receptor-activation-routes proposed. Checked against Du & Lovly 2018, Mishra 2017, Nahta 2012, An 2018, Wu 2022, Lee 2014, Reix 2016, Aquino 2013, Woroniecka 2022 (Europe PMC full text), and Kinde 2011 and Cohen 2017 (read via a page-fetch tool; Cohen read for the first ~100,000 characters). Independent fact-check pass: all 11 citations matched Europe PMC metadata; one unsupported link (MYC "downstream"), a tumour-burden generalisation and an unsourced BCR-ABL1 distractor were removed or reworded.
- **CYP2D6 and Tamoxifen (Ch. 75):** 0 pending claims (9 checked, 2 analogy); missing-context flag (reviewer's "CYP2D6*10, Asians ~50%" is a 58.7% East-Asian *minor allele* frequency, almost exclusive to African/South Asian/East Asian populations, and is an allele frequency rather than metabolizer-phenotype prevalence) and guideline-dependent flag (US/international CPIC plus FDA-labeled dosing; Philippine practice not covered). Fact-check corrected the endoxifen variability figure to Schroth's median 34–52%, reattributed the CYP2D6*10 activity value of 0.25 and the intermediate classification to the 2019 CPIC/DPWG consensus (Caudle 2020) rather than Gaedigk 2018, which records that downgrade as not yet adopted, dropped an unsupported "two large studies dispute it" claim, and qualified the 40 mg dose-escalation evidence. Checked against CPIC 2018 tamoxifen recommendations (quoted in Olivera 2019), Schroth 2017, Gaedigk 2018, Caudle 2017, Caudle 2020, Taylor 2020, Zhou 2017 and Thorén 2021, all read via Europe PMC full text.
- **Biosafety at the Bench: Cabinets, Sharps, HBV Exposure and Sentinel Referral (Ch. 57):** 1 pending claim: Hit 47 select-agent morphology and the reviewer's stand-alone rule that 'routine closed-specimen chemistry follows standard precautions'. Neither is taught. Lesson flags: missing-context (HBV post-exposure management depends on documented response and source HBsAg; the low-titre response-unknown path is HBIG x1 plus revaccination), missing-context (the OSHA recapping ban has a narrow one-handed or mechanical exception), guideline-dependent (US-only OSHA/CDC/ACIP/LRN rules; Philippine rules not covered). Register flags: rv-hbv-pep-vaccinated-hcp, rv-osha-recap-exception, rv-ch57-us-biosafety-scope. Checked against Miller 2012 (CDC Biosafety Blue Ribbon Panel guidelines) and Schillie 2013 (CDC HCP HBV guidance), both read on CDC MMWR web pages; 29 CFR 1910.1030 (eCFR 2026-10-01); ACIP 2018 (Schillie 2018, PMC full text); and the CDC LRN-B web page. A fact-check pass fixed these overstatements: the red flag for a blood culture positive at 48 h or later now requires small gram-negative rods or coccobacilli; aerosols are 'often undetectable', not 'invisible'; reference labs 'test further', not 'confirm'; the recap exception includes a mechanical device.
- **Meningitis CSF: Culture Setup, the Bacterial Pattern, Shunt Infections and the Multiplex Panel (Ch. 66):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's 10% upper bound for CO2 and its Number Vault CSF values; not taught). 2 open lesson flags (f-csf-pattern-gaps and f-shunt-cons-panel, both missing-context). 2 register flags proposed (rv-csf-bacterial-pattern-gaps and rv-vp-shunt-cons-panel-gap). Sources checked:
- **Spectrophotometer Parts: Lamps, Cuvettes, PMT and Wavelength Checks (Ch. 4):** 1 pending claim (reviewer figures not taught: glass 350-2000 nm range and 350 nm quartz cut-off, deuterium ~100 V, ~90 V per dynode, didymium ~600 nm peak, interference-filter bandwidths); lesson flags f-didymium-wavelength (missing-context), f-pmt-figures (uncertain), f-glass-uv-cutoff (method-dependent); register flags rv-ch4-didymium-wavelength-check, rv-ch4-pmt-dynode-figures, rv-ch4-glass-uv-cutoff proposed. PMT gain taught as of the order of a million-fold or more (Michail 2024: order of 10^6; Zheng 2020: more than a million); the hollow cathode is described as plated with the analyte metal; the path-length rule is qualified to the Beer's law range. Checked against Khan 2019, Zacharioudaki 2022, Baral 2026, El Hosry 2023, González-Morales 2020, Whitehead 2017, Zheng 2020, Michail 2024, Reule 1976 and Allen 2007, all read as Europe PMC full text with no page-fetch tool used; independently fact-checked and all citations re-verified with meta.sh.
- **Real M-Spike or Impostor? Immunofixation, Therapeutic Antibodies and Specimen Artifacts (Ch. 47):** 2 pending claims (reviewer's hemolysis band "near transferrin" and "red band visible before staining" in Exam Trap 3; Hit 19's alpha-2 hiding place and "bands between normal proteins may be disregarded"); lesson flags f-tmab-context (missing-context: isatuximab also interferes and has its own shift assay; drug band usually <2 g/L matters at VGPR/CR and with an IgG kappa clone; DIRA is daratumumab-only; mass spectrometry distinguishes several drugs) and f-pseudo-band-causes (missing-context: CRP, iodinated contrast and antibiotics also cause pseudo-peaks; fibrinogen band sits between beta and gamma from residual catheter heparin, and transfer to a thrombin-containing tube removed it in 39/45 samples in one study); register flags rv-tmab-mspike-context, rv-spep-pseudo-band-causes. Beta-region teaching qualified to "can run in beta (especially IgA)" where transferrin and C3 distort the trace. Checked against Caers 2018 (EMN, Haematologica), Lee 2026 (Ann Lab Med), Pires 2025 (J Clin Med), Radocha 2021 (Cancers), van de Donk 2016 (Immunol Rev), Ladwig 2017 (Clin Vaccine Immunol) and Rade 2020 (Biochem Med), all full text via Europe PMC; Jenkins 2009 (Clin Biochem Rev) read via a page-fetch tool but did not cover these artifacts.
- **Blood in Stool: Guaiac vs Immunochemical Tests and the Apt Test (Ch. 23):** 1 pending claim (Number Vault iFOB 50 μg Hb/g, guaiac >5–10 mL/day and 30–50% sensitivity, turnips as interferent; not taught); lesson flags f-ifob-ugib-context (missing-context) and f-gfobt-ppv-restriction (method-dependent); register flags rv-ifob-ugib-context, rv-gfobt-ppv-restriction. Checked against Young 2015 (Dig Dis Sci), Méndez 2025 (BMJ Open Gastroenterol), Salisbury 2021 (Clin Pract Cases Emerg Med), Piccirillo 2024 (Ital J Pediatr) and Kocic 2023 (World J Gastroenterol), all read as Europe PMC full text (no page-fetch tool used). Independent fact-check corrected the takeaway (globin from upper-gut bleeding is digested, not all globin) and softened three overstated cues (FIT instrument reading, causal wording for no restrictions, 'nearly blind' to upper bleeding).
- **Solid-Tumour Drivers: EGFR, ALK, RAS and BRAF V600E (Ch. 78):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's split of exon 19 deletions >50% vs L858R ≈40%, not taught). Lesson flags: extended RAS testing (KRAS and NRAS exons 2–4), not KRAS alone (missing-context); the reviewer's ALK ≈7% is the upper end of 3–7% (uncertain); reviewer BRAF figures differ from the main sources taught: melanoma 66% vs ≈40–60% in Cheng, PTC ≈45% vs 61.7% in TCGA, both inside Crispo's wide ranges (uncertain); colorectal BRAF V600E is treated with a BRAF inhibitor plus an EGFR antibody, unlike melanoma (missing-context). Register flags proposed: rv-ch78-extended-ras, rv-ch78-alk-share, rv-ch78-braf-frequencies, rv-ch78-crc-braf-combination. An independent fact-check pass corrected ROS1/RET denominators (lung adenocarcinoma), fusion-kinase pathway wording, the BRAF-feedback wording and the KRAS frequency attribution. Sources: Sepulveda 2017 (ASCP/CAP/AMP/ASCO colorectal guideline), Chevallier 2021, Majeed 2021, König 2021, Kanat 2020, Cheng 2018, Crispo 2019 and TCGA 2014 (read via a page-fetch tool, first part of the page only). The CAP/IASLC/AMP 2018 lung guideline was not open access and was not read.
- **Amplification Without a Thermal Cycler: TMA, NASBA, LAMP, NEAR and bDNA (Ch. 69):** 1 pending claim (group A strep as an early NEAR CLIA-waived test, plus other Ch. 69 numbers, none taught as fact); 3 lesson flags (NASBA initial-denaturation missing-context, including a commercial TMA assay for hepatitis B virus; bDNA HIV-1 detection limit method-dependent; CLIA waiver US-only guideline-dependent); 3 proposed register flags (rv-tma-nasba-denaturation, rv-bdna-hiv-lod-version, rv-near-clia-us-only). Independent fact-check: the DNA-exclusion point is now taught for NASBA only, and unsourced details (PCR temperatures, 'no amplicons to carry over', 'loosen its folding') were removed. Checked against Dunbar & Das 2019 (J Clin Virol), Obande & Banga Singh 2020 (Infect Drug Resist), Islam & Koirala 2022 (Anal Chim Acta) and Loeffelholz & Tang 2020 (Emerg Microbes Infect), all read as Europe PMC full text, and Notomi 2000 (Nucleic Acids Res) and Elbeik 2000 (J Clin Microbiol), both read via a page-fetch tool.
- **Transplant Viral Loads: CMV Resistance Genes, BK Nephropathy and Integrated HHV-6 (Ch. 64):** 1 pending claim (Number Vault CMV reactivation and adenovirus rates, not taught); 1 lesson flag (f-cmv-ul54-crossres, missing-context: whether UL54 cross-resists foscarnet/cidofovir depends on the mutation; UL97 plus UL54 combined can give high-level ganciclovir resistance; maribavir resistance also maps to UL97; low-grade letermovir mutations also occur in UL51/UL89); 1 register flag proposed (rv-cmv-ul54-crossres). Checked against Kotton 2025 (Fourth International CMV SOT guidelines), Alsanea 2024, Kotton 2024 (Second International BK guidelines) and Pellett 2012, all read as Europe PMC full text (no page-fetch tool used). Independent fact-check qualified overstatements: genotyping at 1000 IU/mL or more is 'more reliable', not required; probable BK is more than 2 weeks; ciHHV-6 prevalence about 1% in US/UK studies; integration screening 'may' prevent needless antivirals.
- **AST Reference Methods, Method Agreement and the Antibiogram (Ch. 58):** 1 pending claim (BMD incubation of 35 °C, ambient air, 16–20 h; attribution of the ≥90% EA criterion to CLSI M52; gradient strip as the practical method for pneumococcal penicillin MICs and fungi; "elliptical" zone wording); 4 open lesson flags (M39-A4 is outdated, now the 5th ed. 2022; the first-isolate rule can underestimate resistance; the M52 attribution of the ≥90% EA criterion could not be checked; BMD incubation conditions not confirmed); 4 register flags proposed (rv-ch58-m39-edition, rv-ch58-first-isolate-underestimate, rv-ch58-ea-m52, rv-ch58-bmd-incubation). Checked against Gajic 2022, Gajdács 2017, Klinker 2021 and Tran 2023 (open full text), and Humphries 2018, Simner 2022 and Horvat 2003 (each read via a page-fetch tool). An independent fact-check corrected one citation, removed one unsupported comparison point and one unsupported explanation detail, and limited the MRSA duplicate figure to the single hospital and year it comes from.
- **Giant Cell Arteritis vs Takayasu Arteritis (Ch. 54):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's figure that 44% of clinically quiet Takayasu patients have active arteritis, the "up to 15%" biopsy false-negative rate, the advice never to stop steroids for a negative biopsy, and the Scandinavian incidence details); 3 lesson flags (biopsy false-negative rate contradicted by a figure of up to 61%; the 4% figure for both ESR and CRP normal applies only to patients not on glucocorticoids, 10.2% including treated patients; the Takayasu age cut-off depends on the guideline, since the 2022 criteria use age ≤60 at diagnosis); 3 register flags proposed (rv-gca-tab-false-negative-rate, rv-gca-normal-markers-steroids, rv-takayasu-age-cutoff). Checked against Kermani 2012 and Maz 2021 ACR/VF guideline (both read via a page-fetch tool, Maz first part of page only), van der Geest 2020, Duftner 2018, Hwang 2026, Tecer 2025, Molina-Collada 2023 and Russo 2018. The 2022 ACR/EULAR criteria papers are not open access and were not read directly.
- **Complement Consumption Patterns (Ch. 48):** 1 pending claim, not taught: the C3NeF "half-life ≥10×" figure, the RA serum-vs-synovial-fluid example, and the idea that acute-phase overproduction holds C3/C4 in the normal range during consumption. 2 open lesson flags, both missing-context: quiescent SLE "normal C4" lacks context, and factor B is an unreliable pathway discriminator. Pickering 2013 does list reduced factor B as an alternative-pathway marker, but Ekdahl 2018 reports it more or less unchanged in C3NeF disease. 2 proposed register flags (rv-sle-c4-quiescent, rv-factor-b-discriminator). Checked against Ekdahl 2018 (Front Immunol), Ayano 2023 (Biomolecules) and the C3 glomerulopathy consensus report (Pickering 2013, Kidney Int), all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used. In the fact-check, a misquote of Pickering and an unsupported synthesis-masking statement were corrected.
- **Bone Marrow Sampling: Site, Dry Tap, Leukoerythroblastic Film and Perls Iron (Ch. 31):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's 0–5+ Perls scale with normal adult 2+ and 5+ overload hook, and the 4–7% dry-tap incidence; neither is taught). Lesson flags: f-perls-scale (method-dependent; also notes that Hit 8's 'ferritin stains' conflicts with Sonoda 2025) and f-leb-causes (missing-context). Register flags proposed: rv-perls-grade-scale and rv-leukoerythroblastic-causes. 7 claims checked against Ridgeway 2017, Hjortholm 2013, Bhaskar 2021, Chew 2024, Langabeer 2025, Sonoda 2025, Phiri 2009 and Lahtiharju 2025, all read as Europe PMC full text. An independent fact-check fixed overstated dry-tap wording (aplastic anaemia added to the causes, 'persistent' qualifier added, and the Q1 rationales corrected), the 'red cells do not turn blue' and 'widely cited Gale scale' wording, and the takeaway heuristic. Bain 2001 (J Clin Pathol) was available only as an abstract through a page-fetch tool and is not cited. The ICSH marrow guideline is not open access and was not read.
- **Waived Molecular POCT: Influenza, Strep A and the Culture-Backup Question (Ch. 7):** 1 pending claim, not taught: five POC molecular flu manufacturers; the Liat GAS 97.2% concordance and 6.6% failure figures; the >$100 cost; operator error specifically causing false negatives. Lesson flags: f-first-waived-molecular (contradiction: Deak and Marlowe 2017 name the Alere i Influenza A&B, 2015, not Roche Liat, as the first CLIA-waived molecular POC test); f-gas-molecular-backup (missing-context: a negative molecular Strep A test \"may not require\" culture, and guidelines do not yet address POC NAAT); f-us-only-wmol (guideline-dependent: Philippine rules not covered). Register flags: rv-first-waived-molecular, rv-gas-molecular-backup. Checked against Deak & Marlowe 2017, Gonzalez & McElvania 2018, Schmitz 2022 and Pellegrino 2023 (Europe PMC full text), and the IDSA Strep A 2012 (Shulman) and influenza 2018 (Uyeki 2019) guidelines. Both guidelines were read via a page-fetch tool, first 100,000 characters only. An FDA press release on the Alere i waiver returned 404. An independent fact-check softened the overstatements: the mnemonic no longer says to skip culture, the sealed-cartridge contamination wording follows the sources, the group C/G wording no longer says 'most', and the takeaway no longer says 'cleared'.
- **Hemolysis, Lipemia and Icterus: Diazo Bilirubin and Ammonia Pitfalls (Ch. 28):** 1 pending claim (RBC ammonia ~3x plasma, cigarette-smoke contamination, reviewer ammonia stability 3 h at 2–4 °C / 24 h at −20 °C, LD1 as the RBC isoenzyme, hemoglobin competing with ferrozine, Number Vault interference limits); 3 lesson flags (hemolysis-iron method-dependent, lipemia direction method-dependent, ammonia stability contradiction); 3 register flags proposed (rv-ch28-hemolysis-iron, rv-ch28-lipemia-direction, rv-ch28-ammonia-stability). Fact-check pass: hemolysis-lowered diazo bilirubin qualified as method-dependent (Ho 2021 did not list total bilirubin among hemolysis-affected assays), in vitro-only rise of potassium/LD/AST/phosphate made explicit, unsupported 'forms glutamate' and 'warmth' wording removed, and Kaplon 2019 (Pract Lab Med) added for the glutamate dehydrogenase method. Checked against Guerra Ruiz 2021 (Adv Lab Med), Thomas 2002 (EJIFCC), Ho 2021 (Pract Lab Med), Nikolac 2014 (Biochem Med), Krasowski 2019 (Acad Pathol), Wan Azman 2019 (Oman Med J), Savy 2018 (Hepat Med), Kaplon 2019 (Pract Lab Med) and Bélanger-Quintana 2022 (Nutrients), all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used.
- **Fat-Soluble Vitamins: Absorption, Vitamin D Status and Vitamin K (Ch. 27):** 1 pending claim covering reviewer statements that are not taught (water-soluble deficiency within weeks, B12 stores 3-6 years, PT rising only after 50% undercarboxylated prothrombin, 1,25(OH)2D "even high" in deficiency, toxicity >500 nmol/L); 2 lesson flags (f-vitd-target guideline-dependent, f-vitd-toxicity missing-context); 2 register flags proposed (rv-vitd-target-guideline, rv-vitd-toxicity-threshold). Checked against Andres 2024, Dirks 2018, Pilz 2019, Amrein 2020, Saponaro 2020, Araki 2020, van Gorp 2015 and Matthaiou 2023, all read as full text via Europe PMC (no page-fetch tool used). An independent fact-check pass qualified overstated wording (1,25(OH)2D "usually" rather than "always" normal; FGF23-mediated disorders give normal-to-low levels), changed "phosphate-wasting" to "hypophosphataemic" disorders, and added Matthaiou 2023 as support for the adult statement that vitamin K deficiency prolongs the PT.
- **BNP and NT-proBNP: Rule-Out Cut-Offs, Confounders and Sacubitril (Ch. 19):** 1 pending claim (reviewer's BNP 100 NPV 89%/PPV 79%, ~22-min BNP half-life, 30–40% week-to-week variability; not taught). Lesson flags: f-bnp-100-rule-out (missing-context) and f-arni-bnp-modest (missing-context). Proposed register flags: rv-bnp-100-rule-out and rv-arni-bnp-modest. Checked against Castiglione 2022 (Heart Fail Rev), Meijers 2021 (HFA-ESC Biomarker Study Group, Eur J Heart Fail) and Nishikimi 2022 (Biology), all read in full text via Europe PMC; no page-fetch tool used. The ESC cut-offs and the Breathing Not Properly/PRIDE figures come from Castiglione 2022's text and tables, and the PARADIGM-HF figures from Nishikimi 2022; the primary studies were not read. An independent fact-check found the citations correct. One fix was made: the mnemonic now says neprilysin does not break NT-proBNP down, replacing the earlier wording that the drug 'cannot touch it'.
- **MALDI-TOF and SELDI Proteomic Patterns: Peaks Without Names (Ch. 79):** 0 pending claims (6 checked, 2 analogy); 1 lesson flag (missing-context: discriminating SELDI peaks can come from non-biologic experimental bias, not only from overfitting); 1 register flag proposed (rv-seldi-bias-context). Checked against Singhal 2015 (Front Microbiol), Ding 2004 (BMC Genet), Grizzle 2005 (Cancer Inform), Sorace and Zhan 2003 (BMC Bioinformatics) and Wegdam 2012 (Proteome Sci), all read as Europe PMC full text with no page-fetch tool. Independent fact-check removed two overstatements: 'proteins usually remain unidentified' (now 'the pattern alone does not identify the proteins') and 'the first ovarian study'. It also qualified the Wegdam accuracy as ovarian cancer versus benign tumours. Not taught: the "24- vs 25-base" wording, the Number Vault SELDI study figures, and Hit 11 (bystander effect / 0.39 correlation).
- **Myeloid Mutations: MPN Drivers, SF3B1, KIT D816V and CHIP (Ch. 77):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's separate CALR type 1/type 2 shares, the MPL 3–5% ET figure, and GIST KIT exon 17 D816V; none of these is taught). Lesson flags: f-mpn-triple-negative (contradiction: ~10% of MPN patients are triple-negative, so "together >95%" overstates coverage) and f-sf3b1-rs-threshold (outdated: the 5%/15% ring-sideroblast rule is WHO 2016; WHO 2022 and ICC 2022 define MDS-SF3B1 by the mutation). Register flags proposed: rv-mpn-triple-negative, rv-sf3b1-rs-threshold. Checked against Khoury 2022 (WHO 5th ed.), Luque Paz 2023, Prins 2020, Lee 2024, Steensma 2015, Arock 2015 (ECNM) and Malcovati 2020; the last three were read via a page-fetch tool. Independent fact-check fixes: KIT is now "codon 816, most often D816V" (not "almost always"), and CHIP is defined by the absence of a blood disorder or unexplained cytopenia rather than by "normal counts". The ICC 2022 primary paper (Arber 2022, PMC9479031) was blocked by a browser-verification page and not read, so the ICC SF3B1 VAF ≥10% rule rests on Lee 2024.
- **Helminth Specimens: Pinworm Tape, Taenia Eggs, Schistosome Spines and Microfilariae Timing (Ch. 65):** 1 pending claim (the "5–10% of pinworm infections in stool" figure, Taenia egg diameter 31–43 μm, Number Vault schistosome egg sizes); 3 open lesson flags (Taenia egg size contradiction, method-dependent S. haematobium urine window, uncertain pinworm stool figure); register flags rv-taenia-egg-size-range, rv-haematobium-urine-window, rv-pinworm-stool-yield-figure proposed. Checked against CDC DPDx (Enterobiasis, Taeniasis, Schistosomiasis, Lymphatic filariasis, Loiasis; all read via a page-fetch tool because direct download returned HTTP 403), Wendt 2019 (read via a page-fetch tool), Ng-Nguyen 2017, Gray 2011, Ponzo 2024 and Pietrzak 2024. An independent fact-check removed overstated wording ("far/much less often" in stool, "tiny" spine, "only" antigen tests), removed an unverified Echinococcus resemblance, and limited the any-time antigen statement to W. bancrofti.
- **Lyme Disease Serology: Two-Tier Testing, the 30-Day IgM Rule and Borrelia mayonii (Ch. 61):** 1 pending claim (tick attachment >24–48 h, bull's-eye frequency, other Number Vault Lyme figures). Lesson flags: early two-tier sensitivity is method-dependent (reviewer ~40–60%; the CDC review gives 30–40% early, with standard two-tier table values of 38–42% acute and 26–61% convalescent, and Branda & Steere cite 14% in solitary EM). Modified two-tier testing is missing (CDC-accepted since 2019). B. mayonii is now also found in New York (2025). Tick-attachment time is uncertain. Testing content is US-only (guideline-dependent). A late isolated IgM blot is taught as 'not evidence of active disease, may be a false positive' rather than 'likely false positive'. Register flags: rv-lyme-early-sensitivity, rv-lyme-mttt-alternative, rv-mayonii-range, rv-lyme-tick-attachment. Checked against Moore 2016 (Emerg Infect Dis), Mead 2019 (MMWR), Branda & Steere 2021 (Clin Microbiol Rev; read via a page-fetch tool, first ~100,000 characters only), Pritt 2016 (Lancet Infect Dis; read via a page-fetch tool), McGowan 2023 (OFID), Nafiz 2026 (MMWR) and Cook 2015 (Int J Gen Med; used only to keep the attachment claim pending). Independently fact-checked; no human expert review.
- **Biologic Therapies: Targets, Risks and the Lab Tests That Guard Them (Ch. 49):** 1 pending claim (TNF blockers in juvenile arthritis; denosumab for tumour osteolysis; vedolizumab PML reports after 2018); lesson flags f-natalizumab-alpha4 and f-benralizumab-il5ra (both missing-context, open); register flags rv-natalizumab-alpha4-chain and rv-benralizumab-il5-receptor proposed. Independent fact-check fixed: an unsupported TNF-contains-TB mechanism removed; 'six to seven' expected vedolizumab PML cases; eosinophil entry criteria attributed to mepolizumab trials (reslizumab ≥400/µL); six-monthly anti-JCV retesting attributed to EMA CHMP; index bands limited to immunosuppressant-naive patients in Q1; PML surveillance mechanism hedged. Checked against Thaler 2009, Kucharz 2021, Faustman 2025, Shim 2014, McGuigan 2016, Glenn 2025, Colombel 2017, Card 2018, Luzentales-Simpson 2021, Mihara 2011, Koike 2009, Zhang 2020 and Gyawali 2025 (all full text via Europe PMC; no page-fetch tool needed).
- **Lipid Cut-Points: Triglyceride Bands, LDL-C 190, Statin Intensity, Metabolic Syndrome and Children (Ch. 18):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's four-band triglyceride scheme <150/150–199/200–499/≥500, and FH suspicion at LDL-C ≥160 under age 20); 2 open lesson flags (f-tg-categories, guideline-dependent: TG bands differ across 2018 AHA/ACC, Endocrine Society and ESC; f-mets-cutoffs, guideline-dependent: waist cut-offs are ethnicity-specific and the 2018 AHA/ACC tally uses TG >175); 2 register flags proposed (rv-tg-categories-scheme, rv-mets-cutoffs-guideline). Checked against Grundy 2019 (2018 AHA/ACC guideline; PMC page read via a page-fetch tool because direct download returned a reCAPTCHA page, and recommendation tables shown as images could not be read), the NHLBI 2011 pediatric panel (read via a page-fetch tool), Hernandez 2021, Kassi 2011 and Nordestgaard 2016 (Europe PMC full text). Independent fact-check removed an unsupported rationale for the LDL-C ≥190 rule and qualified the pancreatitis-treatment, primary-prevention and waist-cut-off statements.
- **Wrong Blood in Tube, Positive Patient ID and Choosing an LIS (Ch. 12):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's list of modules missing from Epic Beaker, the mini-RFP and temporally close demos, the claim that integrated suites squeeze out stand-alone vendors (partly echoed by Tuthill but not taught), and WBIT as the driver of every barcoding standard); lesson flags: uncertain (Beaker module list), missing-context (detecting WBIT and silent ABO-identical errors), guideline-dependent (group-check and acceptance rules vary by country; Philippine rules not covered); register flags rv-lis-integrated-module-gaps and rv-wbit-detection-context proposed. Checked against Ning 2016 (PLoS One), Snyder 2012 (Clin Biochem; read via a page-fetch tool), Sandhu 2017 (J Appl Lab Med; read via a page-fetch tool), Obaidallah 2022 (Transfusion), Aghili Pour 2026 (Transfus Med Hemother), Bolton-Maggs 2013 (Br J Haematol), Tuthill 2014 (J Pathol Inform) and Sergi 2022 (Risk Manag Healthc Policy). Fact-check pass fixed bedside electronic ID being taught as a detection method (it prevents; detection needs a historical or second group), a group-check claim that ignored same-group silent errors, and overstated 'own scripted demo' and API-endorsement wording. The barcode-symbology items (Hits 5-6, Trap 2) are not taught because the GS1 DataMatrix guideline returned 403 and no open source on 1D vs 2D error correction was found.
- **Bone Turnover Markers: Osteoporosis, Osteomalacia and Paget Disease (Ch. 16):** 1 pending claim (explicit "normal mineralization" in osteoporosis, one-third fracture fraction, urinary hydroxyproline as a current Paget test, fixed 50–60% marker fall); 4 open lesson flags (fixed antiresorptive drop, fracture fractions, osteomalacia calcium may be low or normal, Paget ALP/calcium); 4 register flags proposed (rv-btm-antiresorptive-drop, rv-osteoporosis-fracture-fraction, rv-osteomalacia-calcium, rv-paget-alp-calcium). Checked against Schini 2023 (Endocr Rev), Bhattoa 2025 (ESCEO-IOF-IFCC), Gregson 2025 (UK osteoporosis guideline), Uday & Högler 2017, Ralston 2019 (JBMR Paget guideline), Rendina 2024 (SIOMMMS) and Sabharwal 2014, all full text via Europe PMC (no page-fetch tool used). Independent fact-check: citations matched exactly; unsupported "bone pain/proximal weakness" removed, overstated wording ("hallmark", "confirm", "by drug and marker") softened.
- **Respiratory Culture Clues: Otitis Media, Silent Sputum, Pseudomonas and MALDI-TOF (Ch. 66):** 0 pending claims (4 factual claims checked, 2 analogy); lesson flags: Haemophilus influenzae predominates in chronic effusion/recurrent otitis media/treatment failure and overtook pneumococcus after conjugate vaccine in some countries (missing-context), no dominant growth does not prove an atypical pathogen and atypicals need specific tests (missing-context), oxidase is shared by most nonfermenters and in cystic fibrosis Burkholderia cepacia complex needs selective agar and special techniques (missing-context; the grape-odour pairing itself is supported), MALDI-TOF also fails to split closely related species in pure culture (missing-context); register flags: rv-aom-hflu-settings, rv-no-growth-not-atypical, rv-cf-bcc-selective-media, rv-maldi-close-species. Checked against Ngo 2016 (PLoS One), Jamal 2022 (Cureus), Garin 2022 (Microorganisms), Baron 2013 (Clin Infect Dis), Behzadi 2021 (Antibiotics), Scott-Thomas 2010 (BMC Pulm Med), Wood 2023 (Cells), Calderaro 2024 (Microorganisms), Gautam 2017 (Indian J Med Res), all read as Europe PMC full text; an independent fact-check corrected two reference URLs and one citation and narrowed the MALDI rule to one organism per spot rather than mandatory subculture.
- **Gram Stain Steps and the Novobiocin Fork for S. saprophyticus (Ch. 57):** 0 pending claims (5 checked, 2 analogy). Lesson flags: f-novo-unique (missing-context: S. saprophyticus is not the "lone" novobiocin-resistant CoNS; the S. cohnii group and S. hominis subsp. novobiosepticus are resistant too, and mannose and trehalose separate them, not urease) and f-fetch (uncertain: three sources read only through a page-fetch tool). Register flag rv-novobiocin-not-unique proposed. Checked against Li 2020 (Microbiologyopen), Vijayakumar 2023 (J Microsc Ultrastruct), Mitra 2023 (Indian J Ophthalmol) and Lawal 2021 (Emerg Infect Dis), all full text from Europe PMC. Also checked against Becker 2014 (Clin Microbiol Rev; first part and Table 2 only), Vickers 2007 (AAC) and De Paulis 2003 (J Clin Microbiol; Table 1 and footnotes), read via a page-fetch tool. An independent fact-check corrected the confirmation tests from urease to mannose and trehalose.
- **Cellular Immunity Testing: Right Tube, Right Clock, Right Cursor (Ch. 46):** 1 pending claim. It groups five untaught reviewer statements: "preservative-free" heparin, the "<24 h in the dark" storage limit, the 50% biological / 50% technical CD4 variability split, the Loken attribution and blast separation by CD45/SSC, and "no absolute standards" for immunophenotyping reagents. "Keep serial CD4 draws at a consistent time" is taught as an inference from the diurnal studies; no formal same-time or morning-draw guideline was found. Lesson flags: f-lithium-heparin (method-dependent), f-isotype-cursor (outdated), f-diurnal-hiv (missing-context), f-us-only-asr (guideline-dependent). Register flags: rv-cellular-lithium-heparin, rv-isotype-cursor-fmo, rv-cd4-diurnal-hiv-blunted, rv-asr-us-only. Sources checked: the CDC 1997 CD4 guideline (MMWR, full text) and Cossarizza 2021 Eur J Immunol flow guidelines (full-text sections found by search; its EDTA/heparin advice comes from a cytokine-protocol tip). Also the methods sections of Yi 2016, Kruse 2021 and Barabas 2017; the abstracts only of Malone 1990 (truncated; 59 cells/mm3 rise in Walter Reed stage 1-5 patients), Carmichael 2006 and Campbell 1997 (Europe PMC); and the eCFR text of 21 CFR 809.30, 809.10(e), 864.4020 and 42 CFR 493.1253 (version 2026-10-01). Independently re-checked by a second agent, which corrected overgeneralisations. No page-fetch tool was used.
- **Iron Deficiency vs Anemia of Inflammation: Empty or Locked Stores (Ch. 9):** 1 pending claim (the Number Vault RDW interval of 12–17% and RDW cut-offs, not taught); 2 open lesson flags (f-acd-normocytic: the reviewer calls both anemias microcytic, but anemia of inflammation is usually normocytic and only sometimes microcytic; f-ferritin-inflammation: Trap 1 pairs ferritin with RDW, but the sources adjust the ferritin threshold for inflammation and add CRP, TSAT and sTfR/log ferritin); 2 register flags proposed (rv-acd-normocytic-ch9, rv-ferritin-inflammation-ch9). Checked against Nairz 2016 (Wien Med Wochenschr), Iolascon 2024 (Hemasphere), Kumar 2022 (BMJ Open Gastroenterol), Tefferi 2005 (Mayo Clin Proc) and D'Angelo 2013 (Blood Res), all read in full via Europe PMC; no page-fetch tool used. WHO ferritin thresholds (<15 µg/L; <70 µg/L with inflammation) are cited as summarized by Iolascon 2024, because the WHO guideline itself was not opened. An independent fact-check removed an unsupported RDW-overlap statement from the memory aid, corrected a TfR/Tf mix-up and an oversimplified 'ferritin ↑ / transferrin ↓' in the tokens, and fixed the WHO year attributions and one citation.
- **Immunotherapies: Checkpoint Inhibitors, CAR T Cells, Eculizumab and Rituximab (Ch. 44):** 0 pending claims (7 factual claims checked, 2 analogy); 4 open lesson flags (missing-context: the anti-CTLA-4 list leaves out tremelimumab; missing-context: CAR T indications are wider than adult lymphoma and childhood leukaemia, including BCMA CAR T for myeloma; missing-context: eculizumab leaves C3-mediated extravascular hemolysis and meningococcal risk even after vaccination; guideline-dependent: US FDA approvals only, Philippine registration not covered); 4 register flags proposed. Checked against Vaddepally 2020 (Cancers), Li 2025 (Front Immunol, introduction only), Yin 2023 (Front Immunol), Alnefaie 2022 (Front Bioeng Biotechnol), Risitano 2019 (Front Immunol), McNamara 2017 (MMWR) and Salles 2017 (Adv Ther), all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used. An independent fact-check corrected an overstated claim about how often viral vectors are used, swapped 'cytokine storm' for cytokine release syndrome, and replaced an unsupported rationale about PNH flow cytometry.
- **Cutoffs and ROC Curves (Ch. 8):** 1 pending claim: the reviewer's "cutoff whose LR+ best matches LR_threshold" wording, which the lesson does not teach. 1 open lesson flag, f-poscrit-lr (missing-context): the slope of the ROC curve at a point is the density ratio, meaning the likelihood ratio of a result at that value, not the overall LR+; the lesson teaches Nahm's cost-index form instead. 1 register flag proposed (rv-ch8-positivity-criterion-lr). Checked against Nahm 2022 and Çorbacıoğlu & Aksel 2023 (Europe PMC full text) and Hajian-Tilaki 2013 and Leeflang 2013 (read via a page-fetch tool). Fact-check pass corrected the CI rule, which applies to an AUC beating chance, not to comparing tests (that needs a statistically significant difference). It also replaced "equal weight to false positives and false negatives" with "equal weight to sensitivity and specificity" and changed "dozens of methods" to more than 30.
- **Psychiatric CNVs: Schizophrenia Hot Spots, Bipolar Loci and Autism Heritability (Ch. 73):** 1 pending claim: the reviewer's bipolar heritability figure of ">70%", GWAS genes, and CNV loci in other ancestries; none of these is taught. 4 open lesson flags: f-scz-cnv-direction, f-bd-cnv-significance, f-bd-heritability-prevalence, f-asd-prevalence. 4 proposed register flags: rv-ch73-scz-cnv-direction, rv-ch73-bipolar-cnv-significance, rv-ch73-bipolar-heritability-prevalence, rv-ch73-autism-prevalence. Sources and how each was read: Marshall 2017 abstract via Europe PMC, results via a page-fetch tool; Green 2016, Kerner 2014 and Maenner 2023 full text via Europe PMC; Merikangas 2011, Bai 2019 and Miller 2010 abstracts only, because full text was empty. An independent fact-check made these corrections: the microarray blind spots are now qualified as under 1% of causes; 22q11.21 is the largest *finite* odds ratio; the bipolar loci come from a combined analysis, not one UK sample; the schizophrenia loci are "carried by" rather than "explain" 1.4% of cases.
- **Candida Identification: Germ Tube, CHROMagar Colors and Built-In Resistance (Ch. 60):** 1 pending claim: the reviewer's 35°C germ tube incubation is not taught, because the sources read used 37°C. Lesson flags: f-green (missing-context: C. dubliniensis is also green on CHROMagar) and f-lusit (contradiction: C. lusitaniae amphotericin B resistance occurs in some isolates or develops, rather than being intrinsic). Register flags: rv-ch60-chromagar-green-dubliniensis and rv-ch60-lusitaniae-amb-not-intrinsic. Checked against Matare 2017, Marinho 2010, Ahmad 2012, Hospenthal 2006, Yücesoy 2003, Mendoza-Reyes 2022, Douglass 2018 and Vahedi-Shahandashti 2022, all read in full text via Europe PMC (no page-fetch tool used). The fact-check pass replaced a mnemonic copied from the reviewer with an original one, qualified the Hospenthal color generalization, and softened A. terreus to 'most isolates' high amphotericin B MICs. No simulator questions repeat the flagged claims.
- **Array Probe Chemistry: On-Chip Synthesis Yield, Spacers, Density and PNA (Ch. 70):** 2 pending claims (glass ~0.1 pmol/mm2 and 100x polypropylene density, plus yield falling with over-long spacers; PNA rapid hybridization and only-antiparallel recognition); lesson flags f-photolitho-stepwise (outdated: the reviewer's 80-95% per-cycle figure fits early MeNPOC chemistry, about 92-94% coupling, while one maskless study measured about 98.5-99% stepwise coupling, with effective stepwise yield lower than coupling efficiency) and f-pna-direction (contradiction: PNA binding direction is not strictly antiparallel); register flags rv-photolitho-stepwise-yield and rv-pna-binding-direction proposed. Checked against Agbavwe 2011, Sack 2016, Lenoir 2006, Quijano 2017 and D'Agata 2017 (Europe PMC full text) and the Shchepinov 1997 abstract only (Europe PMC; full text not obtained). An independent fact-check removed the unsupported 'no electrostatic repulsion' and 'protease resistance' PNA statements and the claim that 99% coupling gives ~82% full-length 20-mers.
- **Nontuberculous Mycobacteria: Runyon Groups, 30 °C Cultures and Species ID (Ch. 59):** 2 pending claims, neither taught: the M. chimaera "1 in 100 to 1 in 1,000" risk figure, and scotochromogen pigment in light / the M. gordonae group. Lesson flags: f-rapid-grower-boundary (uncertain: sources say rapid growers grow in <7 days on subculture and slow growers in ≥7; the reviewer says ≤7, although its own introduction says "under a week") and f-chimaera-risk-context (missing-context: CDC gives the risk range per patient only in hospitals with ≥1 identified infection). Register flags: rv-rapid-grower-boundary, rv-chimaera-risk-context. The fact-check softened the MALDI-TOF wording: Alcolea-Medina 2019 shows that bead-beating or sonication extraction is not always required, so the lesson now teaches "mycobacteria-specific preparation". Checked against Sharma 2020 (Indian J Med Res), Azadi 2018 (Open Microbiol J), Hashish 2018 (Vet Q), Tsiolakkis 2023 (Microorganisms), Nookeu 2019 (Emerg Infect Dis), Alcolea-Medina 2019 (Sci Rep), Bagnarino 2022 (Microorganisms), Spagnolo 2024 (Pathogens), Sommerstein 2018 (Emerg Infect Dis) and CDC HAN 00397. The ASM practical guidance (Forbes 2018, Clin Microbiol Rev) could not be read: the PMC page showed a browser check, the ASM site returned 403, and only the abstract was available.
- **Autoimmune Liver Disease: AIH Types and Scores, PBC Criteria, PSC Imaging (Ch. 55):** 1 pending claim (whether type 2 AIH is more aggressive; minimal antibody titres in children); 3 open lesson flags: fixed PBC thresholds are guideline-dependent, PSC ALP can be normal (contradiction), and the reported p-ANCA frequency in PSC is uncertain; 3 proposed register flags (rv-pbc-criteria-thresholds, rv-psc-alp-range, rv-psc-panca-frequency). Checked against APASL AIH 2021 (Wang) and PBC 2022 (You) guidance, Terziroli Beretta-Piccoli 2022, BSG/UK-PSC 2019 (Chapman), the Japanese PSC guideline 2017 (Isayama), Palmela 2018 and Jhaveri 2017. The Clin Liver Dis summary of the AASLD 2018 PBC guidance is listed but could not be re-opened on independent fact-check (PMC reCAPTCHA), so it is marked not consulted and verifies nothing.
- **Phagocyte Defects: LAD Types 1 and 2, Chédiak-Higashi, and DHR versus NBT (Ch. 52):** 1 pending claim (dichlorofluorescein diacetate as an alternative burst probe, not taught); 2 open lesson flags (f-lad2-fucose missing-context, f-chs-accelerated missing-context); 2 proposed register flags (rv-lad2-fucose-transporter, rv-chs-accelerated-phase). Checked against Das 2020 (Genes Dis), Kambli 2020 (Front Immunol), Lozano 2014 (Orphanet J Rare Dis) and Yu 2018 (J Pediatric Infect Dis Soc), all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used. Independent fact-check: citations confirmed against Europe PMC metadata; wording corrected so that CHS platelets have reduced (not absent) dense granules, CD18 severity bands are one cohort's classification with Das's genotype caveat, and MPO deficiency can (not always) give an abnormal DHR; three question rationales that went beyond the sources were rewritten.
- **Lupus and RA Serology: Anti-Sm, Anti-dsDNA, Drug-Induced Lupus, RF and Anti-CCP (Ch. 53):** 1 pending claim (reviewer-specific figures: RF positive in 70% of Sjögren syndrome, RF 66–85%/72%, anti-CCP "up to 98%" as a general figure, the 1940/2002 dates, ~15% seronegative RA, antinucleosome/anti-ssDNA in drug-induced lupus); lesson flags f-dsdna-activity-modest (missing-context), f-dil-anti-tnf-dsdna (missing-context), f-ccp-rf-figures (method-dependent); register flags rv-dsdna-activity-modest, rv-dil-anti-tnf-dsdna, rv-ccp-rf-figures proposed. Independent fact-check corrected the anti-Sm "static" wording (sources say only that it is not an activity marker), widened anti-histone in idiopathic SLE to ~30–50%, split the anti-CCP2 and RF pooled analyses, and fixed the anti-CCP specificity range (91.5–98.8% across studies). Checked against Fu 2015 (F1000Res), Pagkopoulou 2025 (J Clin Med), van Beers 2022 (J Transl Autoimmun), Dalle Vedove 2012 (J Dtsch Dermatol Ges), Taylor 2011 (Autoimmune Dis; manufacturer-authored systematic review) and Wu 2021 (Int J Mol Sci), all read as full text via Europe PMC; no page-fetch tool used.
- **Inherited Cancer Genes: MSI and Lynch, RET and MEN 2, BRCA1/2 (Ch. 78):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer defines MSI-H as "instability/LOH"; no source read uses LOH in the MSI-H criterion, so it is not taught). Lesson flags: MSI-H is a screen, not a Lynch diagnosis, because sporadic MLH1 methylation and BRAF V600E tumours are sorted out first (missing-context); mixed NCI panels miss MSH6 deficiency (method-dependent); codon 634 was the most common MEN 2A codon, but routine sequencing now finds more moderate-risk mutations (outdated). Register flags: rv-ch78-msi-screen-sporadic, rv-ch78-ret-634-frequency. Checked against Gallon 2021 (Cancers), Li 2020 (Cancer Cell Int), the Wells 2015 ATA MTC guideline (read via a page-fetch tool, first 200,000 of about 468,000 characters), Raue 2018 (J Endocr Soc), Kim 2021 (Endocrinol Metab) and Levy-Lahad 2007 (Br J Cancer). A fact-check pass removed unsupported wording: "predicts immunotherapy benefit" became "durable responses", the BRCA "surveillance" mentions were dropped, and "most MSI-H CRCs are sporadic" became "many". The Umar 2004 revised Bethesda page on PMC was blocked by a reCAPTCHA check and was not read.
- **Name It, Carry It, Contain It: Specimen ID, Transport and Biosafety (Ch. 66):** 1 pending claim group (the reviewer's "transport media keep organisms alive without multiplying", "eye protection for all specimens", "no growth on sheep blood agar, pinpoint colonies in 3–5 days" and "never pre-label tubes" are not taught as facts); 5 lesson flags (F. tularensis blood-agar growth and timing contradiction; pre-labelling depends on the guideline; irretrievable-specimen exception missing; BSL-3 depends on TB/dimorphic-fungus specimen vs culture stage, with the ASM Francisella guideline itself mixed; US/European-only scope); 4 register flags proposed (rv-ch66-ftularensis-growth, rv-ch66-prelabel-guidance, rv-ch66-unlabeled-irretrievable, rv-ch66-bsl3-specimen-vs-culture). Independent fact-check corrected the lesson: the CDC "open bench is for observation only" rule belongs to the TB laboratory section and is no longer taught as a general BSL-2 rule; suspected Francisella specimens now follow the stricter ASM wording (Class II BSC with BSL-3 precautions); the "holds, not grows" wording for transport media was removed; reject-and-recollect is now supported by Mesganaw 2024. Checked against the ASM sentinel guideline for F. tularensis (2016 PDF, read in full), CDC MMWR 2012 safe-work-practices guideline (CDC web page, full text), Baron 2013 IDSA/ASM, Lippi 2017, Ning 2016, Mesganaw 2024, Hubbard 2011, Gavina 2023 and Carvalho 2014 (full text via Europe PMC), and 42 CFR 493.1242 (eCFR API). The Van Horn 2008 page could not be read because a browser check blocked it; nothing was read via a page-fetch tool.
- **IL-5, Thrombopoietin and What a Count Cannot Show (Ch. 32):** 1 pending claim (platelet lifespan 8–11 days and the 2/3 circulating, 1/3 splenic split; IL-3/kit ligand as megakaryocyte support; giant platelets with micromegakaryocytes in MPN/MDS; eosinophil half-life figures). Lesson flags: MBP killing helminths is shown only in vitro and its in vivo role is uncertain (Hit 12); Charcot-Leyden crystals are nonspecific (Trap 5); TPO is only usually inverse to platelet count and how it is sensed is debated, so the normal-count example is the classic 5q- syndrome subset of MDS with isolated del(5q) (Trap 6). Register flags rv-ch32-mbp-helminth-uncertain, rv-ch32-clc-nonspecific and rv-ch32-tpo-inverse-context proposed. Checked against Rosenberg 2013, Roufosse 2018, Limaye 1990 (abstract only), Su 2018, Thomsen 2023, Kaushansky 2024, Hitchcock 2021, Feng 2015 and Acha 2022 (Europe PMC full text). Aster 1966 is available on PMC only as page images (read via a page-fetch tool, no text), so it was not used. A fact-check pass narrowed the del(5q) platelet statement to the 5q- syndrome subset and removed an unsupported 'then aplastic anaemia' time order.
- **Antibody Binding: Affinity, Avidity, the Precipitin Curve, Monoclonals and Haptens (Ch. 45):** 1 pending claim (polyclonal 'stronger overall avidity', monoclonals distinguishing isoenzymes and isotypes, hapten cross-linking in agglutination, the 'solubilizes' wording, the cyclosporine Ka example, and the IgG red-cell thousandfold figure, which was removed on fact-check); 3 lesson flags (f-scatchard-slope-sign: the slope is -Ka, not Ka; f-precipitin-both-excess: antibody excess also prevents a visible precipitate; f-polyclonal-avidity: the avidity and isoenzyme claims were not found in sources); 3 register flags proposed (rv-scatchard-slope-sign, rv-precipitin-both-excess, rv-polyclonal-avidity). Checked against Lipman 2005 (read via a page-fetch tool; re-read on fact-check), Reverberi 2007 (read by the writer via a page-fetch tool; on fact-check the PMC page gave a captcha and Europe PMC gave 403), Kastritis 2013, Winzor 2026, Fischer 2024, Bachmann 2025, Huber 2013, Moraes 2021, Parray 2020 and Yang 2020. Not read: Ascoli 2018 (tandfonline returned 403) and the NCBI Bookshelf search (captcha). Fact-check fixes: Yang citation set to E986, the unsourced curve-shape limitation and the redissolving-precipitate wording replaced, and two over-certain Q3 rationales qualified.
- **Low-Renin Hypertension: ARR, AVS, Liddle and AME (Ch. 25):** 1 pending claim, not taught: the reviewer's ARR >50 'virtually diagnostic', its AVS '<3:1 = bilateral' cut-off, and oral sodium loading details. 3 lesson flags: f-arr-50 (uncertain), f-arr-pac-min (method-dependent), f-avs-li (method-dependent). 3 register flags proposed: rv-arr-50-diagnostic, rv-arr-pac-minimum, rv-avs-li-cutoffs. Checked against Ha 2023 (Korean Endocrine Society consensus), Romero 2016 (summary of the 2016 Endocrine Society guideline), Quencer 2021, Zhou 2020, Tetti 2018 and Lu 2022, all read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool used. The fact-check pass removed an unsourced 'glycyrrhizic acid', and now says aldosterone is low only in classic AME (Lu 2022 Table 2) and usually low in Liddle syndrome (Tetti 2018: 58.2%). The 2016 and 2025 Endocrine Society guidelines are not open access and were not read.
- **Urine Concentration and Timed Collections (Ch. 29):** 1 pending claim: the reviewer's oliguria <400 mL/day, anuria <100 mL/day and polyuria >2.5 L/day, the "earliest sign of diabetic nephropathy" wording, and a 30–300 mg/L albumin concentration window. None of these were found in the sources read, so they are not taught. 5 lesson flags: (1) 24-h creatinine 20–25 mg/kg/day is the traditional male-only range and dated (women 15–20); (2) the polyuria threshold is contradicted: there is no universal definition, and ≥3 L/24 h or >40–50 mL/kg/24 h is used; (3) the oliguria definition is method-dependent (<0.5 mL/kg/h over 6 h); (4) second-morning urine may be better for cell morphology; (5) albumin categories use ACR or timed excretion, not mg/L. 5 register flags proposed: rv-creatinine-excretion-sex, rv-polyuria-threshold, rv-oliguria-definition, rv-first-morning-formed-elements, rv-albumin-concentration-window. Checked against Simerville 2005 (AAFP page), Caleffi 2010 (journal page), Sacks 2011, McGill 2022, Chagnac 2024, Forni Ogna 2015, Newell-Price 2025 and Klein 2018. StatPearls "Urinalysis" (NCBI Bookshelf) was blocked by a captcha and not read. Sacks 2023 could not be re-read at fact-check (PMC captcha, publisher 403) and is not cited. Fact-check fixes: A2 is now 30–300 mg/g, "most concentrated" was removed, and the under-collection call is hedged.
- **Toxic Alcohols and Salicylate: Reading the Osmolal and Anion Gaps (Ch. 24):** 1 pending claim: the reviewer's "tinnitus flags chronic toxicity" and "use the Done nomogram", neither taught as stated. 3 lesson flags: f-osmolal-gap-normal (missing-context), f-salicylate-tinnitus (missing-context), f-salicylate-done (outdated). 3 register flags proposed: rv-osmolal-gap-normal-not-exclude, rv-salicylate-tinnitus-acute, rv-salicylate-done-nomogram. Checked against Gallagher 2019 (Adv J Emerg Med), Lynd 2008 (BMC Emerg Med), Greene 2020 (Toxicol Rep), Ghannoum 2023 EXTRIP ethylene glycol (Crit Care), Sidlak 2025 (Cureus) and Samlan 2008 (West J Emerg Med). All were read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool was used. An independent fact-check found that Sidlak's 78% tinnitus figure comes from a 1973 therapeutic-dosing study (Mongan 1973, not read), so it is not taught as an acute-overdose figure; acute tinnitus is taught from Samlan 2008 instead. The check also removed an unsupported claim that ethanol is the most common unmeasured osmole, and qualified the isopropanol and lactic acidosis wording. Not read because they are not open access: the EXTRIP salicylate and methanol papers, Dugandzic 1989 (the Done-nomogram study) and Mongan 1973.
- **Gut Hormone Tumours: VIPoma, Gastrinoma and Urine 5-HIAA (Ch. 23):** 1 pending claim (reviewer figures not confirmed: octreotide >90% VIPoma symptom control, stool up to 30 L/day, gastrin >150 ng/L with pH <3 suggestive, urine 5-HIAA >90% sensitivity/specificity); 3 open lesson flags (outdated secretin/gastrin criteria, uncertain 5-HIAA accuracy, uncertain octreotide response); 3 proposed register flags (rv-secretin-gastrin-criteria, rv-5hiaa-accuracy, rv-vipoma-octreotide-response). Checked against Una Cidon 2022, Magi 2023, Franchina 2024, Rossi 2021, Arasaradnam 2018 (BSG), Kerolles 2025 and Azizian 2022 (Europe PMC full text). Ito 2012 is supporting context only: the author read it via a page-fetch tool, but at the independent fact-check the PMC page was blocked by a browser-verification check and only the abstract was re-read, so figures found only in Ito (secretin 94%/100% accuracy, PPI hypergastrinaemia 80-100%, MEN1 20-30%) were removed. The NCBI Bookshelf VIPoma page was blocked by a captcha and not read.
- **Unconjugated vs Conjugated Hyperbilirubinemia, Neurotoxicity and Light (Ch. 22):** 1 pending claim (reviewer figures not taught: hemolysis 1.5–3.0 mg/dL, Gilbert "seldom >5", choledocholithiasis ">90% direct, ALP frequently >300 IU/L", basal-ganglia deposition ">20 mg/dL"). Lesson flags: f-cn1-range (missing-context: the reviewer's Crigler-Najjar I range of "> 5, sometimes > 20" understates the typical 20–50 mg/dL) and f-light-direction (method-dependent: in one diazo study, unprotected plasma showed significantly lower total and direct bilirubin at 48 h, with no significant change up to 8 h, and the shifts between fractions depend on the method; this differs from the reviewer's claim that light generally raises the direct fraction). Register flags: rv-ch22-crigler-najjar-range, rv-ch22-bilirubin-light-direction. Checked against Huang 2022 (Kaohsiung J Med Sci), Guerra Ruiz 2021 (Adv Lab Med), Newsome 2018 (Gut), Morais 2022 (UEG J), Bhutani 2013 (J Clin Neonatol), Zhang 2021 (BMJ Open) and Lindqvist 2025 (Int J Mol Sci), all read in full text via the Europe PMC API. Sofronescu 2012 (Clin Chim Acta) and Ihara 1992 (Clin Chem) were read as Europe PMC abstracts only. The 2022 AAP guideline (Kemper 2022) is not open access and was not read. An independent fact-check corrected the light timing (48 h, not 24–48 h), removed an unsourced "dry-slide" label and an unsupported "most newborn jaundice" claim.
- **Enzymes with One Job: Dibucaine Number, Serum ACE and Acid Phosphatase (Ch. 21):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's fixed DN bands, PChE population frequencies, the "nearly all metastatic cases" ACP figure, Number Vault ACP/ACE figures, and the Löfgren triad with ACE); 4 open lesson flags (f-dibucaine-method-ranges, f-dibucaine-carrier-apnea, f-sarcoid-ace-confounders, f-acp-metastatic-elevation); 4 proposed register flags (rv-dibucaine-method-ranges, rv-dibucaine-carrier-apnea, rv-sarcoid-ace-confounders, rv-acp-metastatic-elevation). Checked against Panhuizen 2010, Jońca 2015, Delacour 2014, Zencirci 2009, Crouser 2020 (ATS guideline), Zheng 2023, Kraaijvanger 2020, Kong 2013, Muniyan 2013 and Xu 2019, all read as Europe PMC full text. An independent fact-check made these fixes: Delacour genotype labels (the DN 58.3 carrier is atypical-Kalow/usual); a low DN now 'suggests' rather than 'means' high risk; sarcoidosis diagnosis 'usually' needs granulomas (ATS: biopsy not always required); PAP and silent-carrier wording; and the Crouser DOI now matches the metadata. No page-fetch tool was needed. The IFCC LD method PDF (Bais 1994) was behind a captcha, so it was not read and not used.
- **Imprinting and Microdeletions: Prader-Willi, Angelman, Williams and WAGR (Ch. 71):** 0 pending claims (all 6 factual claims checked); 3 open lesson flags (missing-context: PWS/AS are not always deletions and the region is 15q11q13; missing-context: Williams-Beuren is a ~1.55 Mb contiguous deletion in a ~28-gene 7q11.23 region that includes ELN, not just an ELN deletion; uncertain: the reviewer's '~1 in 3 children with aniridia develop Wilms' was not found, and sources give 45-60% in WAGR and no risk with isolated PAX6 deletion); proposes register flags rv-pws-as-mechanisms, rv-williams-contiguous-deletion, rv-wagr-aniridia-wilms. Checked against Beygo 2019 (EMQN/ACGS guideline), Angulo 2015, Bird 2014, Honjo 2015, Collins 2024, Stephens 2024, Marcato 2014, Hol 2021 and Duffy 2021, all read in full text via Europe PMC. Kozel 2021 (Nat Rev Dis Primers) could not be read (Europe PMC server error, PMC page blocked by captcha) and is not cited. A fact-check pass removed an unsupported parent-of-origin claim for Williams/WAGR and added a source for the deletion not being visible on karyotype.
- **Mutation Types: Codons, Reading Frame, Point, Repeat, Translocation and Repair Defects (Ch. 68):** 1 pending claim (genetic-code degeneracy, which the sources read do not state, so it is not taught); 3 lesson flags (f-aug-usual-not-always: AUG is the usual start codon, but non-AUG near-cognate start codons are also used; f-dmd-frame-rule-exceptions: close to 10% of dystrophin mutations break the reading-frame rule, and Duchenne can also come from duplications and nonsense point mutations; f-xp-variant-not-ner: the xeroderma pigmentosum variant form, about 30% of cases, has normal nucleotide excision repair and defective DNA polymerase eta); 3 register flags proposed (rv-aug-usual-not-always, rv-dmd-frame-rule-exceptions, rv-xp-variant-not-ner). Checked against Vitale 2025, Kearse 2017, Inusa 2019, Ciaccio 2017, Aartsma-Rus 2016, Sampaio 2021, Gallon 2021 and Leung 2022, all read as Europe PMC full text (no page-fetch tool used); independent fact-check corrected XP wording, an unsupported 5′→3′ reading claim, and two overstatements.
- **Getting Paid for Molecular Tests: Coding, Coverage, Payment and FDA Status (Ch. 67):** 1 pending claim: whether every test without FDA clearance needs a report disclaimer, or only tests built from ASRs; the reviewer's strict coverage → coding → payment order; private-payer rules; the Number Vault and figure (none of these are taught). 4 lesson flags: ASR report-statement scope; billing vs coverage and FDA status; phase order; US-only FDA/CLIA/Medicare rules. 4 proposed register flags: rv-ch67-asr-report-statement-scope, rv-ch67-fda-billing-coverage-context, rv-ch67-reimbursement-phases-order, rv-ch67-fda-payer-us-only. Sources checked: the AMP Economics 101 report (Sireci 2020, full text via Europe PMC); 42 CFR 411.15, 414.507, 414.508 and 493.1253 and 21 CFR 809.30 (eCFR, version 2026-10-01); and the Federal Register final rule 90 FR 45134 (2025), read on govinfo.gov because federalregister.gov returned an access-verification page. The ssa.gov Social Security Act page returned Access Denied and was not used. Fact-check pass: the NGS coverage statement was narrowed to the AMP description (2018 NCD: FDA-approved or cleared NGS companion diagnostics in advanced cancer); an unverified rationale about Medicare paying the fee schedule rather than the lab's charge was replaced; the LDT-rule and CLIA wording was aligned with the source text.
- **Fast Food Poisoning, Colourless MacConkey Colonies, Salmonella Serotypes and H. pylori Tests (Ch. 57):** 0 pending claims (5 checked, 2 analogy); 4 open lesson flags (staphylococcal onset 2–8 h, usually 3–5 h, not 1–6 h; colourless colony plus bloody stool is not specific for Shigella; serotype alone does not link outbreak cases; H. pylori stool antigen is an immunoassay, serology cannot separate active from past infection, and PPI/antibiotic/bismuth washout applies); 4 register flags proposed (rv-staph-food-onset, rv-nlf-bloody-not-shigella, rv-salmonella-serotype-outbreak, rv-hpylori-test-context). Checked against Argudín 2010, Kadariya 2014, Dietrich 2021, IDSA 2017 (Shane; read via a page-fetch tool, first part of the page only), Ud-Din 2014, Kröger 2023, Tickell 2017, Ibrahim 2018, Diep 2019, Smith 2024 (Irish H. pylori consensus), Dore 2021 and Ali 2024 (Europe PMC full text). An independent fact-check made the wording narrower: washout applies to PPIs, not acid suppressants in general, and many EIEC strains, not all, are lactose-negative. The Maastricht VI/Florence consensus was not read because it is not open in PMC.
- **Hepatitis Serology and Pregnancy: Acute HBV, Newborn Prophylaxis, HCV Cure and Rubella Immunity (Ch. 64):** 1 pending claim (Number Vault HAV/HBV/HCV incubation figures and HCV chronicity, not taught). Lesson flags: f-perinatal-hbv-which-infants (missing-context: "vaccine + HBIG within 12 hours" applies to infants of HBsAg-positive mothers and to unknown-status infants under 2,000 g; unknown-status infants of 2,000 g or more get the vaccine within 12 h and HBIG by 7 days if the mother is positive; stable infants of 2,000 g or more born to HBsAg-negative mothers get the vaccine alone within 24 h) and f-us-acip-schedules (guideline-dependent: US ACIP only, Philippine rules not covered). Proposed register flags: rv-perinatal-hbv-which-infants and rv-rubella-postpartum-us-acip. Checked against Schillie 2018 ACIP HBV (Europe PMC full text), McLean 2013 ACIP MMR (CDC MMWR HTML page read directly), Krassenburg 2021 (Europe PMC full text) and the AASLD-IDSA HCV Guidance 2019 (Ghany 2020; read via a page-fetch tool, first 100,000 characters only, because the Europe PMC full text was empty). An independent fact-check confirmed every figure and fixed four wording overstatements (antiviral "suggested", "12 or more weeks", rubella IgG-negative = no serologic evidence of immunity, the HBsAg-negative-mother birth-weight qualifier) and one citation punctuation mismatch.
- **Chlamydia Treatment, NGU and Lymphogranuloma Venereum (Ch. 62):** 3 pending claims (groove sign / painless ulcer wording; 30–50% NGU share and 85–90% asymptomatic men; current FDA status of LGV-specific NAATs and the exact count of extragenital NAATs, with CDC 2021 noting LGV molecular testing was "not widely available or not FDA cleared"); lesson flags f-ct-regimen (outdated, existing rv-ct-regimen, whose appAction "Not taught as treatment in lessons" is now stale because this lesson teaches the 2021 regimen), f-lgv-proctocolitis (missing-context), f-ngu-figures (uncertain), f-us-regulatory (guideline-dependent); proposed register flags rv-ch62-lgv-proctocolitis, rv-ch62-ngu-ct-share, rv-ch62-extragenital-naat-us. Checked against Workowski 2021 (CDC STI Treatment Guidelines, full text via Europe PMC) and the FDA 2019 press release on extragenital NAAT clearance (fetched directly); no page-fetch tool used. Independent fact-check removed unsupported "mucosal" framing and qualified LGV presentation, erythromycin and azithromycin wording.
- **Inside the Analyzer: Tube ID, Carryover, Immunoassay Steps and MALDI-TOF (Ch. 6):** 1 pending claim (disposable tips and probe back-flushing; claim that immunoassay throughput is much lower than chemistry; luminometer detection; MALDI-TOF 90%/98% figures); lesson flags f-ch6-primary-tube (missing-context), f-ch6-maldi-figures (uncertain); register flags rv-ch6-primary-tube-aliquot, rv-ch6-maldi-specificity-figures. Checked against Armbruster 2014 (read via a page-fetch tool; Europe PMC full text returned an error and direct download hit a captcha), Broughton 1984, Li & Bao 2025, Darwish 2006, Hou 2019 and Calderaro 2024. An independent fact-check qualified the aliquoting wording ('should determine'), the S. mitis/oralis naming, the single-colony requirement (direct blood-culture protocols exist), the cumulative-carryover limitation, and the hook's immunoassay-queue causation, which is now left pending.
- **Medium-Vessel Vasculitis: Polyarteritis Nodosa, Hepatitis B and Kawasaki Disease (Ch. 54):** 1 pending claim covers the reviewer's unverified figures: the proteinuria >2 g/day threshold, the ">1/3" historical HBV share of PAN, ">80% between 6 months and 4 years" for Kawasaki disease, the 1.6:1 sex ratio, MI in about 2%, and "10–20% coronary sequelae despite IVIG". There are 3 open lesson flags: f-kd-coronary-after-ivig (contradiction), f-kd-ntprobnp-cutoff (method-dependent) and f-pan-hbv-share (uncertain). Proposed register flags: rv-kd-coronary-after-ivig (needs-verification), rv-kd-ntprobnp-cutoff (verified-issue) and rv-pan-hbv-historic-share (needs-verification). Claims were checked against Wolff 2023 (Int J Mol Sci), Pașa 2023 (Medicina), the Terrier 2020 French recommendations (Orphanet J Rare Dis), Hur 2017 (Korean J Radiol), Gaggar 2024 (Indian J Nephrol), the Lin 2015 meta-analysis (BMJ Open), the Marchesi 2018 Italian guideline (Ital J Pediatr) and Uehara 2012 (J Epidemiol). All were read as Europe PMC full text; no page-fetch tool was used. An independent fact-check pass re-verified every citation and claim, hedged the HBsAg immune-complex mechanism, corrected two rationales and downgraded the coronary flag to needs-verification. The 2012 Chapel Hill paper and the AHA 2017 Kawasaki statement were not read directly.
- **Flow Cytometry Artifacts: Compensation, Tandem Dyes and FMO Controls (Ch. 35):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's 22% PE→PE-Cy5 spillover and the 15%/26% under/over values as general figures; the lesson uses them only as a worked example); 2 lesson flags (f-ch35-comp-value, missing-context; f-ch35-tandem-mechanisms, missing-context); 2 register flags proposed (rv-ch35-compensation-value-specific, rv-ch35-tandem-two-mechanisms). Checked against Cossarizza 2021 (EJI flow guidelines, 3rd ed.), Kalina 2012 (EuroFlow), Roca 2021 (AutoSpill) and Bhowmick 2021 and 2023, all read as Europe PMC full text; McKinnon 2018, read via a page-fetch tool; and the Le Roy 2009 abstract only (full text is not open access). An independent fact-check made these changes: it narrowed the FMO wording to markers of unknown expression and channels with spread, toned down the claims about the biexponential display, and sourced the diagnostic-relevance statement.
- **Molecular CPT Codes: Tiers, 81479 and What the Code Includes (Ch. 67):** 1 pending claim (reimbursement phases, FDA clearance/report disclaimer, payment details not taught); 4 open lesson flags (slide scraping is not microdissection, 81479 scope, 2012 publication vs 2013 Medicare adoption/stacked-code retirement, US-only coding); register flags proposed: rv-ch67-scraping-not-microdissection, rv-ch67-81479-scope, rv-ch67-mopath-codes-2012-2013, rv-ch67-cpt-us-only. Checked against Sireci 2020 (AMP Economics 101, J Mol Diagn, Europe PMC full text), Lynch 2018 (BMC Cancer, Europe PMC full text), CMS Clinical Laboratory Fee Schedule public-use file CY2026 Q4 (CSV descriptors) and the Medicare NCCI Policy Manual Chapter X, revision 1/1/2024 (PDF; the 2025/2026 chapter URLs returned 404). Fact-check pass: Tier 2 level descriptions recast as examples, 81240 wording aligned to the CMS descriptor, bundling statements qualified as Medicare rules starting from sample preparation, and unsupported rationale in the explanation removed. No page-fetch tool used.
- **Opportunistic Fungi: Cryptococcal Induction, Hairy Leukoplakia, Aspergillus Heads and Rhizopus (Ch. 57):** 1 pending claim (A. niger "smooth round conidia", not taught). Lesson flags: f-niger-seriation (contradiction: A. niger is biseriate, not uniseriate; existing register flag rv-niger-seriation), and f-crypto-fluconazole-induction, f-thrush-scrape, f-mucorales-aseptate (missing-context). Register flags proposed: rv-crypto-induction-who2022, rv-ohl-fixed-candidiasis, rv-mucorales-pauciseptate. Checked against Jarvis 2022 (NEJM, AMBITION), Ngan 2022 (Drugs; WHO 2022 regimen table), Harrison 2023 (CID), Khammissa 2016 and Martins 2017 (Int J Dent), Vila 2020 (J Fungi), Stemler 2023 (J Fungi), Samson 2014 and Bian 2022 (Stud Mycol), Silva 2011 (Braz J Microbiol), Gullì 2024 (Diagnostics) and Walther 2019 (J Fungi). All were read in full text through Europe PMC; no page-fetch tool was used. The WHO 2022 guideline itself was not read; its regimen comes from the Ngan 2022 table. An independent fact-check removed the unsupported 'narrow' Aspergillus hyphae wording and changed Mucorales from 'pauciseptate' alone to 'aseptate or pauciseptate'. It also added fluconazole as the induction partner in the trap, the hook answer and the takeaway.
- **Thrombocytopenia Sorting: ITP, Drug-Induced, TTP and Giant Platelets (Ch. 37):** 2 pending claims, both left untaught: the reviewer's HUS 30–100% ADAMTS13 range with the Shiga-toxin HUS age pattern, and increased megakaryocytes in TTP/HIT. Lesson flags f-hus-range and f-megakaryocytes (missing-context); register flags rv-adamts13-hus-range and rv-megakaryocytes-destruction. Taught as checked: ISTH ADAMTS13 cut-offs (<10% hallmark, 10–20% seek alternatives, >20% other diagnoses, >10% consider HUS after excluding secondary TMA), DITP onset usually 5–7 days, and chemotherapy as mainly production failure, since some agents increase platelet destruction (Kuter 2022). The fact-check corrected the chemotherapy overstatement, two Q1 rationales and the Vayne page (E2212). Sources: Zheng 2020 ISTH guideline and Provan 2019 consensus, both read via a page-fetch tool (Provan only the first ~100k of ~400k characters in this check). Also Matsumoto 2023, Oh 2019, Nomura 2016, Wang 2022, Mingot-Castellano 2023, Kuter 2022, Ali 2017, Vayne 2020, Noris 2013 and Balduini 2022, all Europe PMC full text.
- **B-Cell Translocations and the CD10 Trap (Ch. 34):** 1 pending claim (bcl-6 positivity in follicular lymphoma, Hit 9; not taught); 2 lesson flags (f-etv6-late-relapse, f-bcell-not-invariant); 2 register flags proposed (rv-etv6-runx1-late-relapse, rv-bcell-translocation-frequencies). Checked against Alaggio 2022 (WHO-HAEM5 lymphoid), Gajzer & Fromm 2025, Chiaretti 2014, Terwilliger 2017, Sun 2017, Kalisz 2019 and Ladha 2019, all read as Europe PMC full text (no page-fetch tool used). Fact-check pass corrected the FL t(14;18) ~85% framing (share of all FL, not of classic FL), removed an unsupported 'Ki-67 > 95% only in Burkitt' and an unsourced FL Ki-67 contrast, and trimmed unsourced mechanism wording.
- **Secondary Amenorrhea: hCG First, Then FSH to Find the Break (Ch. 26):** 1 pending claim (the reviewer's fixed five-step order as a mandatory sequence, its "bleed within 7 days" wording, and STRAW+10 hormone staging); 2 open lesson flags (amenorrhea fixed sequence, outdated; FSH 85 labelled menopause without age context, missing-context); 2 register flags proposed (rv-amenorrhea-fixed-sequence, rv-fsh-menopause-vs-poi). Checked against Gibson 2020 (J Clin Res Pediatr Endocrinol, adolescent FHA review), Nelson 2023 (Front Endocrinol) and the ESHRE 2024 POI guideline (Panay 2024, Hum Reprod Open), all read as Europe PMC full text, and the WHO menopause fact sheet (read with curl). Independent fact-check: the progestin challenge is now described as "may be unreliable" (the source's wording), and the FSH-not-high causes are restricted to those in Nelson's table (thyroid removed). The STRAW+10 executive summaries on PMC were reported blocked by a browser verification page and were not read.
- **Alternative Specimens: Salivary Cortisol, Meconium, β2-Transferrin and Matrix Validation (Ch. 30):** 1 pending claim: the general "meconium EtG documents maternal alcohol use" wording, β2-transferrin accuracy figures, the CLSI C49-A text (not read because it is not open access) and the "most sensitive screen" framing. 2 open lesson flags, both missing-context: β2-transferrin is not CSF-only, and the meconium window's onset is imprecise and the EtG statement unqualified. Register flags rv-b2-transferrin-not-csf-only and rv-meconium-window-onset. Independent fact-check corrections: licorice is now qualified as a theoretical, not proven, cause of false-positive late-night salivary cortisol (Nieman 2008); EtG is now framed as a third-trimester marker from one cohort (Maschke 2021); "costly and slow" is now attributed to Oh 2017, against Normansell 1994, which called its 3.5-hour run fast. Checked against Nieman 2008 Endocrine Society guideline (read via a page-fetch tool, first 100,000 characters), Lange 2014, Price 2018, Maschke 2021, de Campos 2022, Oh 2017, Normansell 1994 (abstract-level page read via a page-fetch tool) and the Croatian extravascular body-fluid recommendations Parts I and II (2020). PMC1721515 was blocked by a browser verification page and not read.
- **GFR Markers: Inulin, Creatinine Clearance and the BUN Ratio (Ch. 15):** 4 pending claims (direction of bilirubin and cephalosporin interference in the Jaffe assay; "muscle wasting cuts production to <1/5 of predicted"; tube feeding plus muscle wasting as the top inpatient cause of a high BUN/Cr ratio; inulin reference values 127/118 mL/min/1.73 m²); lesson flags f-inulin-reference (missing-context) and f-bun-ratio (missing-context); register flags rv-inulin-reference-context and rv-bun-cr-ratio-prerenal. Checked against Speeckaert 2021, Ebert 2021, Seegmiller 2020, Bischoff 2014, Pottel 2024, Wang 2024, Biljak 2017 (KDIGO 2012 table as reproduced there), Drion 2012, Ostermann 2016, Husain 2023 and Uchino 2012. All were read as full text from Europe PMC; no page-fetch tool was used. An independent check replaced 'tube feeding' and 'protein load' with Uchino's 'parenteral nutrition, protein catabolism' as the influences on BUN, and qualified the Husain collection-error attribution.
- **Antiplatelet Drugs, CYP2C19 and Guided Therapy (Ch. 39):** 1 pending claim (boxed-warning date March 2010, genotype-specific CYP2C9/VKORC1 dose percentages, warfarin resistance dose; not taught); 3 open lesson flags (warfarin genotype trials taught as mixed, contradicting "no meaningful TTR improvement"; aspirin recovery time taught as method-dependent, with both the 5-7-day review figure and the 96-hour study finding; FDA boxed-warning date unverified and US-only); 3 register flags proposed (rv-warfarin-genotype-trials, rv-aspirin-recovery-time, rv-clopidogrel-boxed-warning).
